\displaystyle H_{{\mathbf{0}}}(t)\leq C_{d}\sigma t(\log n)^{(d+2)/2}+C_{d}\sigma t(\log n)^{d/2}\log\Big(1+\frac{M}{t}\Big)+C_{d}\sigma t(\log n)^{5d/8}+C_{d}\sigma M^{1/4}t^{3/4}n^{1/8}.

Also, by analogous arguments, the same upper bound holds for
H_{\delta}(t)
for
\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
.
Thus, in this case, the inequality
h(t_{2})\leq 0
holds for

t_{2}=\max\Big(\sigma,C_{d}\sigma(\log n)^{(d+2)/2},C_{d}\sigma(\log n)^{d/2}\cdot\log\Big(1+\frac{M}{\sigma}\Big),C_{d}\sigma(\log n)^{5d/8},C_{d}\sigma^{4/5}M^{1/5}n^{1/10}\Big).

Consequently, using Theorem
7
and Proposition
6
, we can derive that

\begin{split}R_{F}(\hat{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,1},f^{*})&=\frac{1}{n}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|\hat{\theta}_{T}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\big]\leq\frac{C}{n}\cdot(\sigma^{2}+t_{2}^{2})\\
&\leq C_{d}\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}M^{1/2}}{n}\Big)^{4/5}+C_{d}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}(\log n)^{d}\bigg[\log\Big(\Big(1+\frac{M}{\sigma}\Big)n\Big)\bigg]^{2}+C_{d}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}(\log n)^{5d/4}.\end{split}

(60)

We now show that
M=M(g^{*})
in the bounds (
59
) and (
60
) can be replaced with
V_{\text{design}}(f^{*})
.
Fix
c_{S}\in{\mathbb{R}}
for each subset
S\subseteq[d]
with
1\leq|S|\leq s
,
and let
A:[0,1]^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
denote the multi-affine function defined as

A(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=g^{*}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})-\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}c_{S}\prod_{k\in S}x_{k}\qquad\text{for $(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1]^{d}$.}

Next, define
\widebar{g}=g^{*}-A\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
,
and let
\widebar{y}_{\mathbf{i}}=y_{\mathbf{i}}-A(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})})
for
\mathbf{i}\in I_{0}
.
It then follows that

\widebar{y}_{\mathbf{i}}=y_{\mathbf{i}}-A(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})})=f^{*}(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})})-A(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})})+\xi_{\mathbf{i}}=g^{*}(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})})-A(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})})+\xi_{\mathbf{i}}=\widebar{g}(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})})+\xi_{\mathbf{i}}

(61)

for
\mathbf{i}\in I_{0}
.
We can thus view
(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})},\widebar{y}_{\mathbf{i}})
for
\mathbf{i}\in I_{0}
as observations from the model (
61
), where the true regression function is
\widebar{g}
.
Moreover, define
\widebar{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}=\hat{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}-A
.
Since
{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
is invariant under subtraction by
A
, i.e.,

{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}=\big\{f-A:f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}\big\},

it is clear that
\widebar{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}
is a least squares estimator over
{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
, based on observations
(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})},\widebar{y}_{\mathbf{i}})
for
\mathbf{i}\in I_{0}
.
That is,

\widebar{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}\in\mathop{\rm argmin}_{f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}}\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in I_{0}}\big(\widebar{y}_{\mathbf{i}}-f(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})})\big)^{2}.

Hence, the bounds (
59
) and (
60
) hold for
R_{F}(\widebar{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s},\widebar{g})
with
M=M(\widebar{g})
.
Since

R_{F}(\hat{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s},f^{*})=R_{F}(\hat{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s},g^{*})=R_{F}(\widebar{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s},\widebar{g}),

this implies that the bounds (
59
) and (
60
) also hold for
R_{F}(\hat{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s},f^{*})
with
M=M(\widebar{g})
.

Observe that, for each subset
S\subseteq[d]
with
1\leq|S|\leq s
,

\begin{bmatrix}0,t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $\widebar{g}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}0,t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}-c_{S}\qquad\text{ for every }t_{k}>0\text{ for }k\in S

and

\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},1,&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $\widebar{g}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},1,&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}-c_{S}\qquad\text{ for every }t_{k}<1\text{ for }k\in S.

Hence, if we choose
c_{S}
as

c_{S}=\inf\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},1,&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}<1\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}

for each subset
S
, then we have

\displaystyle M(\widebar{g})=\max_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\bigg(

\displaystyle\sup\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}0,t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}>0\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad-\inf\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},1,&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}<1\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}\bigg)

(recall the definition of
M(\cdot)
from (
55
)).

Since
g^{*}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
, there exist real numbers
\beta_{0}
and
\beta_{S}
(for
1\leq|S|\leq s
) and finite measures
\nu_{S}
(for
1\leq|S|\leq s
) on
[0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
such that

g^{*}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\beta_{0}+\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}\bigg[\prod_{k\in S}x_{k}\bigg]-\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\int_{[0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}}\bigg[\prod_{k\in S}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\bigg]\,d\nu_{S}(t_{k},k\in S)

for
(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1]^{d}
.
Observe that, for each
S
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}}\prod_{k\in S}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{S}(t_{k},k\in S)

\displaystyle=\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k})\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[t_{k},x_{k})}1\,d(s_{k},k\in S)\,d\nu_{S}(t_{k},k\in S)

\displaystyle=\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k})}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,s_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}}1\,d\nu_{S}(t_{k},k\in S)\,d(s_{k},k\in S)

\displaystyle=\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k})}\nu_{S}\bigg(\prod_{k\in S}[0,s_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\bigg)\,d(s_{k},k\in S).

Thus, it can be readily verified that, for each
S
,

\begin{bmatrix}0,t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}=\beta_{S}-\frac{1}{\prod_{k\in S}t_{k}}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,t_{k})}\nu_{S}\bigg(\prod_{k\in S}[0,s_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\bigg)\,d(s_{k},k\in S)

for every
t_{k}>0
for
k\in S
, and

\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},1,&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}=\beta_{S}-\frac{1}{\prod_{k\in S}(1-t_{k})}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[t_{k},1)}\nu_{S}\bigg(\prod_{k\in S}[0,s_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\bigg)\,d(s_{k},k\in S)

for every
t_{k}<1
for
k\in S
.
Since
\nu_{S}
is nonnegative, this implies that

\sup\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}0,t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}>0\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}\leq\beta_{S}

and

\inf\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},1,&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}<1\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}\geq\beta_{S}-\nu_{S}\big([0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\big).

As a result, we have

M(\widebar{g})\leq\max_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\nu_{S}\big([0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\big)\leq\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\nu_{S}\big([0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\big)=V(g^{*}).

Recall that the bounds (
59
) and (
60
) hold for
R_{F}(\hat{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s},f^{*})
with
M=M(\widebar{g})
.
Also, recall that
g^{*}
is any function in
{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
that agrees with
f^{*}
at the design points
\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})}
.
Hence, the bounds (
59
) and (
60
) still remain valid when
M
is replaced with
V_{\text{design}}(f^{*})
.
∎

C.3.3
Proof of Theorem
3

The following theorem, from
Bellec [5]
, is a key tool for our proof of Theorem
3
.
Recall the model (
46
) and the definition (
47
) of the least squares estimator
\hat{\theta}_{K}
over a closed convex subset
K
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Theorem
9
provides an alternative bound for the expected squared error
{\mathbb{E}}[\|\hat{\theta}_{K}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}]
of
\hat{\theta}_{K}
.
The bound in Theorem
9
is expressed in terms of the
statistical dimension
of the
tangent cone
of
K
, which are defined as follows.

For a closed convex subset
K
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, the tangent cone of
K
at
\theta\in K
is defined as

{\mathcal{T}}_{K}(\theta)=\overline{\{t(\eta-\theta):t\geq 0\text{ and }\eta\in K\}},

where
\overline{\{\cdot\}}
denotes the closure.
It is clear that
{\mathcal{T}}_{K}(\theta)
is closed and convex.
Also, it can be readily checked that
{\mathcal{T}}_{K}(\theta)
is a cone, meaning that if
\zeta\in{\mathcal{T}}_{K}(\theta)
and
t\geq 0
, then
t\zeta\in{\mathcal{T}}_{K}(\theta)
.
For a closed convex cone
{\mathcal{T}}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, the statistical dimension of
{\mathcal{T}}
is defined by

\delta({\mathcal{T}})={\mathbb{E}}\|\Pi_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{2}^{2},

where
\mathbf{Z}=(Z_{1},\dots,Z_{n})
is a Gaussian random vector with
Z_{i}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}N(0,1^{2})
,
\Pi_{{\mathcal{T}}}(\mathbf{Z})
denotes the projection of
\mathbf{Z}
onto
{\mathcal{T}}
, and
\|\cdot\|_{2}
is the Euclidean norm.
It is well-known that the statistical dimension
\delta(\cdot)
is
monotone: for closed convex cones
{\mathcal{T}}\subseteq{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
, we have

\delta({\mathcal{T}})\leq\delta({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
(see, e.g.,
Amelunxen et al. [2, Proposition
3.1]
).

Theorem 9

(Corollary 2.2 of
Bellec [5]
)
.

Under the model (
46
),
\hat{\theta}_{K}
(defined in (
47
)) satisfies that

{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|\hat{\theta}_{K}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\big]\leq\inf_{\theta\in K}\big\{\|\theta-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\cdot\delta({\mathcal{T}}_{K}(\theta))\big\}.

We also use the following notation, which extends
T
defined in (
49
), in our proof of Theorem
3
.
For positive integers
m_{1},\dots,m_{d}
, we define

T(m_{1},\dots,m_{d})=\bigg\{\Big(f\Big(\frac{i_{1}}{m_{1}},\dots,\frac{i_{d}}{m_{d}}\Big),(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})\in I(m_{1},\dots,m_{d})\Big):f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}\bigg\},

where
I(m_{1},\dots,m_{d})
is the index set defined as in (
51
).
Note that
T=T(n_{1},\dots,n_{d})
.

Furthermore, as in the proof of Theorem
1
, we re-index
(\mathbf{x}^{(1)},y_{1}),\dots,(\mathbf{x}^{(n)},y_{n})
as
(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})},y_{\mathbf{i}})
for
\mathbf{i}\in I_{0}
in our proof of Theorem
3
.
For each real-valued function
f
on
[0,1]^{d}
, we also denote by
\theta_{f}
the vector of evaluations of
f
at the design points:

\theta_{f}=(f(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})}),\mathbf{i}\in I_{0}).

Proof of Theorem
3
.

Recall that
\theta^{*}=(f^{*}(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})}),\mathbf{i}\in I_{0})=\theta_{f^{*}}
, and
\hat{\theta}_{T}
is defined as the least squares estimator over
T
:

\hat{\theta}_{T}=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\theta\in T}\|\mathbf{y}-\theta\|_{2},

where
\mathbf{y}=(y_{\mathbf{i}},\mathbf{i}\in I_{0})
.
Also, recall that we have

R_{F}(\hat{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s},f^{*})=\frac{1}{n}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|\hat{\theta}_{T}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\big].

Since
T=\{\theta_{f}:f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}\}
, applying Theorem
9
with
K=T
, we obtain

\displaystyle R_{F}(\hat{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s},f^{*})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|\hat{\theta}_{T}-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\big]\leq\inf_{\theta\in T}\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\cdot\|\theta-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\cdot\delta({\mathcal{T}}_{T}(\theta))\Big\}

\displaystyle=\inf_{f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}}\Big\{\|f-f^{*}\|_{n}^{2}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\cdot\delta({\mathcal{T}}_{T}(\theta_{f}))\Big\}\leq\inf_{f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}\cap\mathcal{R}^{d,s}}\Big\{\|f-f^{*}\|_{n}^{2}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}\cdot\delta({\mathcal{T}}_{T}(\theta_{f}))\Big\}.

Hence, to prove Theorem
3
, it suffices to show that

\delta({\mathcal{T}}_{T}(\theta_{f}))\leq\begin{cases}C_{d}\cdot m(f)\cdot(\log n)^{(5d+6s-3)/4}&\text{if }s\geq 2,\\
C_{d}\cdot m(f)\cdot(\log n)^{\max(d+2,5d/4)}&\text{if }s=1\end{cases}

for every
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}\cap\mathcal{R}^{d,s}
.

Fix
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}\cap\mathcal{R}^{d,s}
, and let

P=\bigg\{\prod_{k=1}^{d}\big[u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1},u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}\big]:j_{k}=1,\dots,m_{k}\bigg\}

be an axis-aligned split of
[0,1]^{d}
with
|P|=m(f)
such that
0=u_{0}^{(k)}<\dots<u_{m_{k}}^{(k)}=1
for
k=1,\dots,d
, and
f
is a multi-affine function of interaction order
s
on each rectangle of
P
.
For each
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|I_{0}|}
and
\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}[m_{k}]
, let
\theta^{(\mathbf{j})}
be the subvector of
\theta
defined as

\theta^{(\mathbf{j})}=\Big(\theta_{i_{1},\dots,i_{d}},\Big(\frac{i_{1}}{n_{1}},\dots,\frac{i_{d}}{n_{d}}\Big)\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}\big[u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1},u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}\big)\Big).

Also, for each
\mathbf{j}
, by shifting indices, we re-index
\theta^{(\mathbf{j})}
with
I(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})},\dots,n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})})
, where
n_{k}^{(\mathbf{j})}
is the number of
i_{k}
’s for which
i_{k}/n_{k}\in[u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1},u^{(k)}_{j_{k}})
.

Now, we claim that

{\mathcal{T}}_{T}(\theta_{f})\subseteq\bigg\{\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|I_{0}|}:\theta^{(\mathbf{j})}\in T\big(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})},\dots,n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})}\big)\text{ for every }\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}[m_{k}]\bigg\}:={\mathcal{T}}_{P}.

It is clear that
{\mathcal{T}}_{P}
is closed, and if
\theta\in{\mathcal{T}}_{P}
and
t\geq 0
, then
t\theta\in{\mathcal{T}}_{P}
.
For the claim, it is thus enough to show that, for every
\theta\in T
, we have
\theta-\theta_{f}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{P}
.
Fix
\theta\in T
, and choose any function
g\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
with
\theta_{g}=\theta
.
Also, fix
\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}[m_{k}]
, and, for each
k=1,\dots,d
, let
l_{k}^{(\mathbf{j})}
be the smallest integer for which
l_{k}^{(\mathbf{j})}/n_{k}\in[u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1},u^{(k)}_{j_{k}})
.
For these fixed
\theta
and
\mathbf{j}
, we show that
(\theta-\theta_{f})^{(\mathbf{j})}=\theta_{g-f}^{(\mathbf{j})}\in T(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})},\dots,n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})})
, from which the claim follows directly.

Suppose
f
is of the form

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\beta_{0}^{(\mathbf{j})}+\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}^{(\mathbf{j})}\bigg[\prod_{k\in S}x_{k}\bigg]

(62)

on
\prod_{k=1}^{d}[u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1},u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}]
, for some constants
\beta_{0}^{(\mathbf{j})}
and
\beta_{S}^{(\mathbf{j})}
,
1\leq|S|\leq s
.
First, let
f_{0}:[0,1]^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
be the function that is of the form (
62
) over the entire domain
[0,1]^{d}
.
Note that while
f
may not be a multi-affine function on
\prod_{k=1}^{d}[l_{k}^{(\mathbf{j})}/n_{k},(l_{k}^{(\mathbf{j})}+n_{k}^{(\mathbf{j})})/n_{k}]
, the function
f_{0}
is multi-affine on
\prod_{k=1}^{d}[l_{k}^{(\mathbf{j})}/n_{k},(l_{k}^{(\mathbf{j})}+n_{k}^{(\mathbf{j})})/n_{k}]
by definition.
Next, define
h
as

h(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=(g-f_{0})\Big(\frac{l_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})}+n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})}\cdot x_{1}}{n_{1}},\dots,\frac{l_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})}+n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})}\cdot x_{d}}{n_{d}}\Big)

for
(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1]^{d}
.
Observe that

\Big(\frac{l_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})}+n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})}\cdot x_{1}}{n_{1}},\dots,\frac{l_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})}+n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})}\cdot x_{d}}{n_{d}}\Big)\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}\big[u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1},u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}\big)

provided that
x_{k}\leq(n_{k}^{(\mathbf{j})}-1)/n_{k}^{(\mathbf{j})}
for
k=1,\dots,d
.
It thus follows that

\theta_{g-f}^{(\mathbf{j})}=\theta_{g-f_{0}}^{(\mathbf{j})}=\bigg(h\Big(\frac{i_{1}}{n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})}},\dots,\frac{i_{d}}{n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})}}\Big),(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})\in I\big(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})},\dots,n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})}\big)\bigg).

We now verify that
h\in{\mathcal{F}^{d}_{\text{TCP}}}
and satisfies the interaction restriction condition (
13
) for all subsets
S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|>s
.
By the definition of
T(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})},\dots,n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})})
and (the proof of) Proposition
6
, this implies that
(\theta-\theta_{f})^{(\mathbf{j})}=\theta_{g-f}^{(\mathbf{j})}\in T(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})},\dots,n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})})
, which proves the claim.
To see this, first, note that the divided differences of
f_{0}
of order
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\dots,p_{d})
with
\max_{j}p_{j}=2
are all zero because
f_{0}
is a multi-affine function.
Combining this with the fact that
g\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}\subseteq{\mathcal{F}^{d}_{\text{TCP}}}
, it follows that
h\in{\mathcal{F}^{d}_{\text{TCP}}}
.
Also, it is straightforward to see that the interaction restriction condition (
13
) for
h
can be derived from those for
g
and
f_{0}
, which are from the fact that
g,f_{0}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
.

Due to the monotonicity of the statistical dimension
\delta(\cdot)
, we have

\delta({\mathcal{T}}_{T}(\theta_{f}))\leq\delta({\mathcal{T}}_{P})={\mathbb{E}}\|\Pi_{{\mathcal{T}}_{P}}(\mathbf{Z})\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{\mathbf{j}\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}[m_{k}]}{\mathbb{E}}\|\Pi_{T(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})},\dots,n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})})}(\mathbf{Z}^{(\mathbf{j})})\|_{2}^{2}.

Note that
{\mathbb{E}}\|\Pi_{T(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})},\dots,n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})})}(\mathbf{Z}^{(\mathbf{j})})\|_{2}^{2}/(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})}\cdots n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})})
is equal to the risk
R_{F}(\hat{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s},f^{*})
of the estimator
\hat{f}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}
,
when
f^{*}=0
,
\sigma=1
, and the design points are
(i_{1}/n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})},\dots,i_{d}/n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})})
for
(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})\in I(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})},\dots,n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})})
.
Hence, we can reuse the results established in Theorem
1
to derive that

\delta({\mathcal{T}}_{T}(\theta_{f}))\leq\sum_{\mathbf{j}\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}[m_{k}]}C_{d}\cdot\Big(\log\big(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})}\cdots n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})}\big)\Big)^{(5d+6s-3)/4}\leq C_{d}\cdot m(f)\cdot(\log n)^{(5d+6s-3)/4}

when
s\geq 2
,

\delta({\mathcal{T}}_{T}(\theta_{f}))\leq\sum_{\mathbf{j}\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}[m_{k}]}C_{d}\cdot\Big(\log\big(n_{1}^{(\mathbf{j})}\cdots n_{d}^{(\mathbf{j})}\big)\Big)^{\max(d+2,5d/4)}\leq C_{d}\cdot m(f)\cdot(\log n)^{\max(d+2,5d/4)}

when
s=1
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

C.3.4
Proof of Theorem
4

Our proof of Theorem
4
almost follows the proof of Theorem 3.5 of
Ki et al. [34]
.
As in that proof, the following theorem, which is a version of
Han and Wellner [28, Proposition 2]
, plays a central role in our proof of Theorem
4
.

Theorem 10

(Theorem 11.12 of
Ki et al. [34]
)
.

Suppose we are given
(\mathbf{x}^{(1)},y_{1}),\dots,(\mathbf{x}^{(n)},y_{n})
generated according to the model

y_{i}=f^{*}(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})+\xi_{i},

where
\mathbf{x}^{(i)}
are i.i.d. random variables with law
P
on
[0,1]^{d}
, and
\xi_{i}
are i.i.d. error terms independent of
\mathbf{x}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{x}^{(n)}
, with mean zero and finite
L^{q}
norm for some
q\geq 3
.
The function
f^{*}:[0,1]^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
is an unknown regression function to be estimated.
Let
{\mathcal{F}}
denote a collection of continuous real-valued functions on
[0,1]^{d}
, and assume
f^{*}\in{\mathcal{F}}
.
Also, assume that
\|f-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M
for all
f\in{\mathcal{F}}
.
Let
\hat{f}
be an estimator of
f^{*}
satisfying

\hat{f}\in\mathop{\rm argmin}_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(y_{i}-f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\big)^{2}\bigg\}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon
for some
\epsilon\geq 0
.

Suppose there exists
t_{n}>0
such that the following inequalities hold for every
r\geq 1
:

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\ \text{ and }\ {\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}.

Here,
\|\cdot\|_{P,2}
is the norm defined by

\|f\|_{P,2}=\big({\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{X}\sim P}[f(\mathbf{X})^{2}]\big)^{1/2},

and
\epsilon_{i}
are i.i.d. Rademacher variables independent of
\mathbf{x}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{x}^{(n)}
.
Under these assumptions, there exists a universal positive constant
C
such that, for every
t\geq t_{n}
,

{\mathbb{P}}(\|\hat{f}-f^{*}\|_{P,2}>t)\leq\epsilon+C\cdot\frac{1+M^{3}}{t^{3}}\cdot t_{n}^{3}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+M^{3}}{n^{3/2}t^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}(\|\xi_{1}\|_{q}^{3}n^{3/q}+M^{3})}{n^{3}t^{6}}.

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{mars}}}
denote the collection of all functions of the form
(
9
) where
\nu_{S}
is a finite signed (Borel) measure on

[0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
for each subset
S\subseteq[d]
with
1\leq|S|\leq s
.
As discussed in Section
7
, this is the function class studied in
Ki et al. [34]
.
For this proof, we use the results established for
{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{mars}}}
in
[
34
]
.

First, using the same argument as in the proof of Lemma 11.15 of
Ki et al. [34]
, we can prove the following:

Lemma 12
.

The regularized variant
\hat{f}_{\text{TC},V}^{d,s}
satisfies that

\|\hat{f}_{\text{TC},V}^{d,s}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(1).

Fix
\epsilon>0
.
This lemma guarantees that, there exists
M>0
such that

{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{f}_{\text{TC},V}^{d,s}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M\big)\geq 1-\epsilon

(63)

for sufficiently large
n
.
Define

{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)=\big\{f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}:V(f)\leq V\text{ and }\|f-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M\big\},

and

{\mathcal{F}}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{M}(V)=\big\{f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{mars}}}:V(f)\leq V\text{ and }\|f-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M\big\}.

It is clear that
{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)
is a collection of continuous functions on
[0,1]^{d}
and contains
f^{*}
.
Also, from (
63
) and the definition of
\hat{f}_{\text{TC},V}^{d,s}
, it follows that, for sufficiently large
n
,

\hat{f}_{\text{TC},V}^{d,s}\in\mathop{\rm argmin}_{f}\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(y_{i}-f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\big)^{2}:f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)\bigg\}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon
.

Since
{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}\subseteq{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{mars}}}
, we have
{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)\subseteq{\mathcal{F}}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{M}(V)
.
It follows that, for every
t>0
,

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

and

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg],

where
\epsilon_{i}
are i.i.d. Rademacher variables independent of
\mathbf{x}^{(i)}
.
Thus, by repeating the arguments in the proof of Theorem 3.5 of
Ki et al. [34]
, we can obtain

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\ \ \text{ and }\ \ {\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}

for every
r\geq 1
, provided that

\displaystyle t_{n}=(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})\bigg[C_{B,d}(1+M^{1/2})^{4/5}\big[(1+M^{1/2})^{1/5}+(M+V)^{1/5}\big]+C_{B,d}M^{4/9}V^{1/9}\bigg[1+\Big(\frac{M+V}{1+M^{1/2}}\Big)^{4/45}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+C_{B,d}(1+M^{1/2})^{4/5}V^{1/5}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{Vn^{1/2}}{1+M^{1/2}}\Big)\bigg]^{4(s-1)/5}\bigg]\cdot n^{-2/5}.

By using Theorem
10
with the function class
{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)
, we can show that

{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{f}_{\text{TC},V}^{d,s}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>t\big)\leq\epsilon+C\cdot\frac{1+M^{3}}{t^{3}}\cdot t_{n}^{3}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+M^{3}}{n^{3/2}t^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}(\|\xi_{1}\|_{5}^{3}n^{3/5}+M^{3})}{n^{3}t^{6}}

for sufficiently large
n
and
t\geq t_{n}
.
As a result, again, by repeating the arguments in the proof of Theorem 3.5 of
Ki et al. [34]
, we can find
K>0
for which

{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{f}_{\text{TC},V}^{d,s}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>Kn^{-2/5}(\log n)^{4(s-1)/5}\big)<2\epsilon

for sufficiently large
n
.
This proves that

\|\hat{f}_{\text{TC},V}^{d,s}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}=O_{p}\big(n^{-4/5}(\log n)^{8(s-1)/5}\big).

∎

C.3.5
Proof of Theorem
5

We use the following variant of Theorem
10

(
[
34
, Theorem 11.12]
) to prove Theorem

5
. The only differences are that the errors are
now assumed to be sub-exponential, and the term
\|\xi_{1}\|_{q}^{3}n^{3/q}
in the
probability bound is replaced by
K^{3}[\log(2+n\sigma^{2}/K^{2})]^{3}
as a
result of that stronger assumption. Below, we describe the modifications
needed in the proof of Theorem
10
to establish Theorem

11
.

Theorem 11
.

Suppose we are given

(\mathbf{x}^{(1)},y_{1}),\dots,(\mathbf{x}^{(n)},y_{n})
generated
according to the model

y_{i}=f^{*}(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})+\xi_{i},

where
\mathbf{x}^{(i)}
are i.i.d. random variables with law
P
on

[0,1]^{d}
, and
\xi_{i}
are i.i.d. mean-zero sub-exponential errors,
independent of
\mathbf{x}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{x}^{(n)}
and
satisfying (
24
) for some
K,\sigma>0
. The function

f^{*}:[0,1]^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
is an unknown regression function to be
estimated. Let
{\mathcal{F}}
denote a collection of continuous real-valued functions
on
[0,1]^{d}
, and assume
f^{*}\in{\mathcal{F}}
. Also, assume that

\|f-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M
for all
f\in{\mathcal{F}}
. Let
\hat{f}
be an
estimator of
f^{*}
satisfying

\hat{f}\in\mathop{\rm argmin}_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(y_{i}-f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\big)^{2}\bigg\}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon
for some
\epsilon\geq 0
.

Suppose there exists
t_{n}>0
such that the following inequalities hold
for every
r\geq 1
:

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\ \text{ and }\ {\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}.

Here,
\|\cdot\|_{P,2}
is the norm defined by

\|f\|_{P,2}=\big({\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{X}\sim P}[f(\mathbf{X})^{2}]\big)^{1/2},

and
\epsilon_{i}
are i.i.d. Rademacher variables independent of

\mathbf{x}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{x}^{(n)}
.
Under these assumptions, there exists a universal positive constant
C

such that, for every
t\geq t_{n}
,

{\mathbb{P}}(\|\hat{f}-f^{*}\|_{P,2}>t)\leq\epsilon+C\cdot\frac{1+M^{3}}{t^{3}}\cdot t_{n}^{3}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+M^{3}}{n^{3/2}t^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}\{K^{3}(\log(2+n\sigma^{2}/K^{2}))^{3}+M^{3}\}}{n^{3}t^{6}}.

Remark 2
.

In the proof of Theorem
10
, the term

\|\xi_{1}\|_{q}^{3}n^{3/q}
in the probability bound arises as an upper bound
(up to a constant) on
{\mathbb{E}}[\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{3}]
under the moment assumption

\|\xi_{i}\|_{q}<\infty
for some
q\geq 3
. Under the stronger assumption
that
\xi_{i}
are sub-exponential random variables satisfying (
24
)
for some
K,\sigma>0
, we can obtain a sharper bound for

{\mathbb{E}}[\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{3}]
as follows.

Define
\psi:{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
by

\psi(x)=\exp(x^{1/3})-1-x^{1/3}-\frac{1}{2}\cdot x^{2/3}.

It is straightforward to check that
\psi
is strictly increasing, convex, and
invertible. Hence,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{3}\big]

\displaystyle=K^{3}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\max_{i}\Big|\frac{\xi_{i}}{K}\Big|^{3}\Big]=K^{3}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\psi^{-1}\Big(\max_{i}\psi\Big(\Big|\frac{\xi_{i}}{K}\Big|^{3}\Big)\Big)\Big]\leq K^{3}\psi^{-1}\bigg({\mathbb{E}}\Big[\max_{i}\psi\Big(\Big|\frac{\xi_{i}}{K}\Big|^{3}\Big)\Big]\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq K^{3}\psi^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\psi\Big(\Big|\frac{\xi_{i}}{K}\Big|^{3}\Big)\Big]\bigg)=K^{3}\psi^{-1}\bigg(n{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\psi\Big(\Big|\frac{\xi_{1}}{K}\Big|^{3}\Big)\Big]\bigg)\leq K^{3}\psi^{-1}\Big(\frac{n\sigma^{2}}{2K^{2}}\Big),

where the first inequality uses the concavity of
\psi^{-1}
, and the
last inequality follows from

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\psi\Big(\Big|\frac{\xi_{1}}{K}\Big|^{3}\Big)\Big]\leq{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\exp\Big(\Big|\frac{\xi_{1}}{K}\Big|\Big)-1-\Big|\frac{\xi_{1}}{K}\Big|\bigg]\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2K^{2}}.

Let
C>0
be a constant such that

\psi(x)\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\exp(x^{1/3})\ \text{ for }x\geq C.

Then, for
x\geq\psi(C)
, we have

\psi^{-1}(x)\leq[\log(2x)]^{3}.

Consequently,

\psi^{-1}\Big(\frac{n\sigma^{2}}{2K^{2}}\Big)\leq C+\Big[\log\Big(1+\frac{n\sigma^{2}}{K^{2}}\Big)\Big]^{3}\leq C\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{n\sigma^{2}}{K^{2}}\Big)\Big]^{3},

from which it follows that

{\mathbb{E}}\big[\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{3}\big]\leq CK^{3}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{n\sigma^{2}}{K^{2}}\Big)\bigg]^{3}.

Recall that in this section, the value of
C
may vary from line
to line.

By using this sharper bound for
{\mathbb{E}}\big[\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{3}\big]
, we
can replace the term
\|\xi_{1}\|_{q}^{3}n^{3/q}
in the probability bound of
Theorem
10
with
K^{3}[\log(2+n\sigma^{2}/K^{2})]^{3}

and establish Theorem
11
.

Proof of Theorem
5
.

Fix
\epsilon_{0}>0
. By the same argument as in the proof of Theorem

4
, there exists
M>0
such that

{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{\text{TC},V}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M\big)\geq 1-\epsilon_{0}

for sufficiently large
n
.
Define

{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)=\big\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}:V(f)\leq V\text{ and }\|f-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M\big\}

and

B_{M,p_{0}}(t)=\big\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}:\|f-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M\text{ and }\|f-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\big\}.

Then,
f^{*}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)
, and for sufficiently large
n
, we have

\hat{f}_{\text{TC},V}^{d,s}\in\mathop{\rm argmin}_{f}\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(y_{i}-f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\big)^{2}:f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)\bigg\}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon_{0}
.

We first consider the special case
f^{*}=0
.
In this case,

{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)=\big\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}:V(f)\leq V\text{ and }\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M\big\}

and

B_{M,p_{0}}(t)=\big\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}:\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M\text{ and }\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\big\}.

Our goal is to apply Theorem
11
.
To this end, we need to bound

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg].

(64)

Since the Rademacher variables
\epsilon_{i}
are also sub-exponential, the same
bound applies to

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg].

We first enlarge the function class over which the supremum is taken:

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{f\in B_{M,p_{0}}(t)}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg].

(65)

By the same argument as in the proof of Lemma 11.17 of
[
34
]
,
the expectation on the right-hand side of (
65
) can be
bounded in terms of the bracketing entropy integral:

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{f\in B_{M,p_{0}}(t)}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq CJ_{[\ ]}(t,B_{M,p_{0}}(t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\cdot\Big(\sigma+KM\cdot\frac{J_{[\ ]}(t,B_{M,p_{0}}(t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}{t^{2}\sqrt{n}}\Big),

(66)

where

J_{[\ ]}(t,B_{M,p_{0}}(t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})=\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{1+\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B_{M,p_{0}}(t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}\,d\epsilon,

and
N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|)
denotes the

\epsilon
-bracketing number of
\mathcal{G}
with respect to
\|\cdot\|
.
Since
b\leq p_{0}(x)\leq B
for all
x\in[0,1]^{d}
, we also have

N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B_{M,p_{0}}(t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon/B^{1/2},B_{M}(t/b^{1/2}),\|\cdot\|_{2}),

(67)

where

B_{M}(t)=\big\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}:\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M\text{ and }\|f\|_{2}\leq t\big\}.

For
\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}
, define

P^{(\delta)}=\prod_{k=1}^{d}P_{\delta_{k}},

where
P_{0}=[0,1/2]
and
P_{1}=[1/2,1]
.
Also, let
B_{M}^{(\delta)}(t)
denote the collection of functions

g:P^{(\delta)}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
g=f|_{P^{(\delta)}}
for some

f\in B_{M}(t)
.
It then follows that

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B_{M}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2})\leq\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{2^{d/2}},B_{M}^{(\delta)}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big).

(68)

From this point forward, we focus on bounding

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B_{M}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2})
.
An entirely analogous argument applies to

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B_{M}^{(\delta)}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2})
for

\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
.
Fix
\epsilon>0
, and define

R=\max\bigg(\bigg\lceil\log\Big(\frac{dM^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big)/\log 2\bigg\rceil-(d-3),1\bigg).

This choice of
R
ensures that

\frac{M^{2}}{2^{d-2}}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{R}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}.

Now, consider the set

P^{({\mathbf{0}})}_{\text{int}}=\Big[\frac{1}{2^{R+1}},\frac{1}{2}\Big]^{d},

and let
B_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t)
denote the collection of functions

g:P_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that

g=f|_{P_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}}
for some
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}

with
\|f\|_{2}\leq t
.
Intuitively,
P_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}
can be thought of as an interior of

P^{({\mathbf{0}})}=[0,1/2]^{d}
obtained by peeling off its boundary.
We claim that

\log N_{[\ ]}\big(\epsilon,B_{M}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2}\big)\leq\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2}},B_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big).

(69)

To prove this, let
g\in B_{M}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t)
. By definition,

g=f|_{P^{({\mathbf{0}})}}
for some
f\in B_{M}(t)
, and hence,

g|_{P_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}}\in B_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t)
.
Let
[h_{1},h_{2}]
be a bracket containing
g|_{P_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}}
with

\|h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon/\sqrt{2}
.
Consider extensions
h_{1}^{\text{ext}}
and
h_{2}^{\text{ext}}
of
h_{1}
and
h_{2}

to
P^{({\mathbf{0}})}
for which
h_{1}^{\text{ext}}(x)=-M
for

x\notin P_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}
and
h_{2}^{\text{ext}}(x)=M
for

x\notin P_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}
.
Then,
g\in[h_{1}^{\text{ext}},h_{2}^{\text{ext}}]
and

\displaystyle\|h_{1}^{\text{ext}}-h_{2}^{\text{ext}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+4M^{2}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{2^{d}}-\Big(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2^{R+1}}\Big)^{d}\bigg)\leq\|h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{M^{2}}{2^{d-2}}\cdot\Big(1-\Big(1-\frac{1}{2^{R}}\Big)^{d}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\|h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{M^{2}}{2^{d-2}}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{R}}\leq\|h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}.

This proves the claim (
69
).

Next, we split
P_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}
into dyadic pieces.
Let
\mathcal{R}=\{1,\dots,R\}^{d}
, and for each

\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\mathcal{R}
, define

Q^{(\mathbf{r})}=\prod_{k=1}^{d}Q_{r_{k}},

where
Q_{l}=[1/2^{l+1},1/2^{l}]
for
l=1,\dots,R
.
Also, for each
\mathbf{r}\in\mathcal{R}
, let

B_{\mathbf{r}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t)
denote the collection of functions

g:Q^{(\mathbf{r})}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
g=f|_{Q^{(\mathbf{r})}}
for some

f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}
with
\|f\|_{2}\leq t
.
Since

P_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{r}\in\mathcal{R}}Q^{(\mathbf{r})},

we have

\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2}},B_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)\leq\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in\mathcal{R}}\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2|\mathcal{R}|}},B_{\mathbf{r}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big).

(70)

We now apply the following lemma, proved in Appendix

C.5.2
, to bound each term on the right-hand
side of (
70
).

Lemma 13
.

Assume that
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}
and
\|f\|_{2}\leq t
.
Then, for every
\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[d]
,

1/2^{r_{k}+1}\leq s_{k}<t_{k}\leq 1/2^{r_{k}}
for
k\in S
, and

t_{k}\in[1/2^{r_{k}+1},1/2^{r_{k}}]
for
k\notin S
, we have

\begin{bmatrix}s_{k},t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
t_{k},&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}\geq-C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k\in S}r_{k}}

(71)

if
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, and

\begin{bmatrix}s_{k},t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
t_{k},&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}\leq C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k\in S}r_{k}}

(72)

if
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{d}
, where

r_{+}=\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}
.

Fix
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\mathcal{R}
, and
suppose
g\in B_{\mathbf{r}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t)
. Define

h:[0,1]^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

h(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=g\Big(\frac{x_{1}+1}{2^{r_{1}+1}},\dots,\frac{x_{d}+1}{2^{r_{d}+1}}\Big),

so that
h
is a domain-scaled version of
g
.
By the definition of
B_{\mathbf{r}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t)
, there exists

f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}
with
\|f\|_{2}\leq t
such that

g=f|_{Q^{(\mathbf{r})}}
. Since
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}
, it follows
that
h\in{\mathcal{F}^{d}_{\text{TCP}}}
, and
h
satisfies the interaction restriction condition
(
13
) (see also (
32
) and
(
33
)) for every subset
S
of
[d]
with
|S|>s
.
By Lemma
13
, for every nonempty subset

S
of
[d]
,

\displaystyle\sup\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}0,t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $h$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\\
\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}>0\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\prod_{k\in S}\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}+1}}\cdot\sup\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}1/2^{r_{k}+1},t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
1/2^{r_{k}+1},&k\notin S&\\
\end{bmatrix}:\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}+1}}<t_{k}<\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}}}\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}\leq\frac{1}{2^{|S|}}\cdot C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}

and

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},1,&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $h$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}<1\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\prod_{k\in S}\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}+1}}\cdot\inf\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},1/2^{r_{k}},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
1/2^{r_{k}+1},&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}+1}}<t_{k}<\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}}}\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}\geq-\frac{1}{2^{|S|}}\cdot C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}.

Hence, Proposition
2
ensures

h\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
.
Also,
V(h)
of
h
can be bounded as

\displaystyle V(h)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\emptyset\neq S\subseteq\{1,\dots,d\}}\bigg(\sup\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}0,t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $h$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\\
\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}>0\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\inf\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},1,&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $h$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}<1\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\emptyset\neq S\subseteq\{1,\dots,d\}}\frac{1}{2^{|S|}}\cdot C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\leq C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}.

Moreover,

\|g\|_{2}^{2}=\int_{Q^{(\mathbf{r})}}\big(g(x)\big)^{2}\,dx=\prod_{k=1}^{d}\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}+1}}\cdot\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\big(h(x)\big)^{2}\,dx=\frac{1}{2^{r_{+}+d}}\cdot\|h\|_{2}^{2}.

Consequently, it follows that

\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2|\mathcal{R}|}},B_{\mathbf{r}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)\leq\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{2^{(r_{+}+d-1)/2}\epsilon}{|\mathcal{R}|^{1/2}},D(C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2},t\cdot 2^{(r_{+}+d)/2}),\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big),

where

D(V,t):=\big\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}:V(f)\leq V\text{ and }\|f\|_{2}\leq t\big\}.

The following lemma, proved in Appendix
C.5.3
,
provides an upper bound on the bracketing number of
D(V,t)
for
V,t>0
.

Lemma 14
.

There exists a positive constant
C_{d}
depending on
d
such that

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,D(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{2})\leq C_{d}\log\Big(2+\frac{t+V}{\epsilon}\Big)+C_{d}\Big(2+\frac{V}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{2(s-1)}

for every
V,t>0
and
\epsilon>0
.

Applying Lemma
14
, we obtain

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2|\mathcal{R}|}},B_{\mathbf{r}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{d}\log\Big(2+\frac{C_{d}t|\mathcal{R}|^{1/2}}{\epsilon}\Big)+C_{d}\Big(2+\frac{C_{d}t|\mathcal{R}|^{1/2}}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{C_{d}t|\mathcal{R}|^{1/2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{2(s-1)}.

Combining this with (
69
) and (
70
), we
deduce

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}\big(\epsilon,B_{M}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2}\big)\leq\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2}},B_{\text{int}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)\leq\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in\mathcal{R}}\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2|\mathcal{R}|}},B_{\mathbf{r}}^{({\mathbf{0}})}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{d}|\mathcal{R}|\cdot\log\Big(2+\frac{C_{d}t|\mathcal{R}|^{1/2}}{\epsilon}\Big)+C_{d}|\mathcal{R}|\cdot\Big(2+\frac{C_{d}t|\mathcal{R}|^{1/2}}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{C_{d}t|\mathcal{R}|^{1/2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{2(s-1)}.

Next, by (
68
),

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B_{M}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2})\leq\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{2^{d/2}},B_{M}^{(\delta)}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{d}|\mathcal{R}|\cdot\log\Big(2+\frac{C_{d}t|\mathcal{R}|^{1/2}}{\epsilon}\Big)+C_{d}|\mathcal{R}|\cdot\Big(2+\frac{C_{d}t|\mathcal{R}|^{1/2}}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{C_{d}t|\mathcal{R}|^{1/2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{2(s-1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{d,M}\log\Big(2+\frac{C_{d,M}t}{\epsilon}\cdot\Big(\log\Big(2+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)^{d/2}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(2+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{d}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C_{d,M}\Big(2+\frac{C_{d,M}t}{\epsilon}\cdot\Big(\log\Big(2+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)^{d/2}\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(2+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{d}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{C_{d,M}t}{\epsilon}\cdot\Big(\log\Big(2+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)^{d/2}\Big)\bigg]^{2(s-1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{d,M}\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{d}+C_{d,M}\log\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{d}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C_{d,M}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{2(s-1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C_{d,M}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4}\bigg(\log\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{2(s-1)},

where the third inequality uses

|\mathcal{R}|=R^{d}\leq C_{d,M}\bigg(\log\Big(2+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{d},

which follows from

R\leq\max\Big(\log\Big(\frac{dM^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big)/\log 2-(d-2),1\Big)\leq C_{d,M}\log\Big(2+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big).

If
t\geq 1
, then

\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\leq\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big).

If instead
t<1
, then

\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)=\log\Big(3t+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)-\log t\leq\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)+\log\frac{1}{t}\leq\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)+\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big).

Thus, in all cases,

\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\leq\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)+\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big).

As a result,

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B_{M}(t),\|\cdot\|_{2})

\displaystyle\leq C_{d,M}\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)+\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{d+1/e}

\displaystyle\qquad+C_{d,M}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)+\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{2(s-1)}

\displaystyle\qquad+C_{d,M}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)+\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4+2(s-1)/e}

\displaystyle\leq C_{d,M}\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{d+1/e}+C_{d,M}\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{d+1/e}

\displaystyle\qquad+C_{d,M}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4+2(s-1)}

(73)

\displaystyle\qquad+C_{d,M}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{2(s-1)}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4+2(s-1)/e}.

For the first inequality, we also used
\log\log x\leq(\log x)^{1/e}

for all
x>1
.
By (
67
), it follows that

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B_{M,p_{0}}(t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq C_{b,B,d,M}\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{d+1/e}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C_{b,B,d,M}\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{d+1/e}+C_{b,B,d,M}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4+2(s-1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C_{b,B,d,M}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{2(s-1)}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4+2(s-1)/e}.

Next, we bound the integrals appearing in the bracketing entropy integral.
Observe that

\int_{0}^{t}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{d/2+1/2e}\,d\epsilon\leq Ct,

\int_{0}^{t}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{d/2+1/2e}\,d\epsilon\leq Ct\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{d/2+1/2e},

\int_{0}^{t}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/4}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/8+s-1}\,d\epsilon\leq Ct,

and

\int_{0}^{t}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/4}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{s-1}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/8+(s-1)/e}\,d\epsilon\leq Ct\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/8+(s-1)/e}.

Hence, the bracketing entropy integral satisfies

J_{[\ ]}(t,B_{M,p_{0}}(t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})=\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{1+\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B_{M,p_{0}}(t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}\,d\epsilon\leq C_{b,B,d,M}t\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{a(d,s)},

where

a(d,s):=\max\Big(\frac{5d}{8}+\frac{s-1}{e},\frac{d}{2}+\frac{1}{2e}\Big).

Combining this with (
65
) and (
66
)
gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{f\in B_{M}(t)}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq CJ_{[\ ]}(t,B_{M}(t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\cdot\Big(\sigma+KM\cdot\frac{J_{[\ ]}(t,B_{M}(t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}{t^{2}\sqrt{n}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C_{b,B,d,M}\sigma t\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{a(d,s)}+C_{b,B,d,M}Kn^{-1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{2a(d,s)}:=\Psi(t).

Let

t_{n}=C_{b,B,d,M}(1+\sigma+K^{1/2})\cdot n^{-1/2}\big(\log(3+n^{1/2})\big)^{a(d,s)}.

Then, we have

\Psi(t_{n})\leq\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}.

Since
t\mapsto\Psi(t)/t
is decreasing, for every
r\geq 1
,

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq\Psi(rt_{n})\leq r\Psi(t_{n})\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}.

Repeating all of the preceding arguments with the Rademacher variables

\epsilon_{i}
in place of
\xi_{i}
also yields

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}

for every
r\geq 1
.

Applying Theorem
11
therefore gives

{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>t\big)\leq\epsilon_{0}+C\cdot\frac{1+M^{3}}{t^{3}}\cdot t_{n}^{3}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+M^{3}}{n^{3/2}t^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}K^{3}[\log(2+n\sigma^{2}/K^{2})]^{3}+M^{6}}{n^{3}t^{6}}.

It follows that for each
L>0
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>Ln^{-1/2}(\log n)^{a(d,s)}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\epsilon_{0}+\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\bigg[C\cdot\frac{(1+M^{3})t_{n}^{3}}{L^{3}n^{-3/2}(\log n)^{3a(d,s)}}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+M^{3}}{L^{3}(\log n)^{3a(d,s)}}+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}K^{3}[\log(2+n\sigma^{2}/K^{2})]^{3}+M^{6}}{L^{6}(\log n)^{6a(d,s)}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\epsilon_{0}+C_{b,B,d,M}\cdot\frac{(1+\sigma+K^{1/2})^{3}}{L^{3}},

where the second inequality uses that
6a(d,s)>3d\geq 3
.
Hence, for sufficiently large
L
,

\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>Ln^{-1/2}(\log n)^{a(d,s)}\big)<2\epsilon_{0},

which proves that

\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}=O_{p}\big(n^{-1/2}(\log n)^{a(d,s)}\big).

Note that the constants underlying
O_{p}
depend on
b,B,d,V,K
, and

\sigma
, with the dependence on
V
entering through
M
.

We now prove the theorem for the general case.
Suppose that
f^{*}
is multi-affine of the form (
21
) on
each rectangle

\mathcal{U}^{(\mathbf{j})}:=\prod_{k=1}^{d}\big[u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1},u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}\big],\quad\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}\{1,\dots,n_{k}\},

where
0=u^{(k)}_{0}<\dots<u^{(k)}_{n_{k}}=1
for
k=1,\dots,d
.
Also, assume that
\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}=m(f^{*})=:m
.
As in the special case
f^{*}=0
, our goal is to apply Theorem
11
.
To this end, we aim to bound

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg].

As before, we first enlarge the function class:

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{f\in B_{M,p_{0}}(t)-\{f^{*}\}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg].

Analogous to (
66
), this expectation can bounded
using the bracketing entropy integral as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{f\in B_{M,p_{0}}(t)-\{f^{*}\}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(\mathbf{x}^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq CJ_{[\ ]}(t,B_{M,p_{0}}(t)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Big(\sigma+KM\cdot\frac{J_{[\ ]}(t,B_{M,p_{0}}(t)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}{t^{2}\sqrt{n}}\Big).

Since
b\leq p_{0}(x)\leq B
for all
x\in[0,1]^{d}
, we also have

N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B_{M,p_{0}}(t)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon/B^{1/2},\widebar{B}_{M}(t/b^{1/2})-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{2}),

(74)

where

\widebar{B}_{M}(t)=\big\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}:\|f-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M\text{ and }\|f-f^{*}\|_{2}\leq t\big\}.

Next, we decompose
[0,1]^{d}
into
m
rectangles
\mathcal{U}^{(\mathbf{j})}
,
on which
f^{*}
is multi-affine.
For each
\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}\{1,\dots,n_{k}\}
,
let
A_{\mathbf{j}}
denote the area of
\mathcal{U}^{(\mathbf{j})}
:

A_{\mathbf{j}}=\prod_{k=1}^{d}\big(u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}-u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1}\big),

and let
\widebar{B}^{(\mathbf{j})}_{M}(t)
be the collection of functions

g:\mathcal{U}^{(\mathbf{j})}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that

g=f|_{\mathcal{U}^{(\mathbf{j})}}
for some
f\in\widebar{B}_{M}(t)
.
It then follows that

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\widebar{B}_{M}(t)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{2})\leq\sum_{\mathbf{j}\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}\{1,\dots,n_{k}\}}\log N_{[\ ]}\big(\epsilon/\sqrt{m},\widebar{B}^{(\mathbf{j})}_{M}(t)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{2}\big).

(75)

Recall that
m=m(f^{*})=\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}
.

Suppose
g\in\widebar{B}^{(\mathbf{j})}_{M}(t)
.
By the definition of
\widebar{B}^{(\mathbf{j})}_{M}(t)
, there exists

f\in\widebar{B}_{M}(t)
with
\|f-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M
and

\|f-f^{*}\|_{2}\leq t
such that
g=f|_{\mathcal{U}^{(\mathbf{j})}}
.
Define
h:[0,1]^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

h(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=(g-f^{*})\Big(u^{(1)}_{j_{1}-1}+\big(u^{(1)}_{j_{1}}-u^{(1)}_{j_{1}-1}\big)x_{1},\dots,u^{(d)}_{j_{d}-1}+\big(u^{(d)}_{j_{d}}-u^{(d)}_{j_{d}-1}\big)x_{d}\Big).

Since
f,f^{*}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
and
f^{*}
is multi-affine on

\mathcal{U}^{(\mathbf{j})}
, it follows that
h\in{\mathcal{F}^{d}_{\text{TCP}}}
, and
h
satisfies
the interaction restriction condition (
13
) (see also
(
32
) and (
33
)) for every
subset
S
of
[d]
with
|S|>s
. By induction, it can also be readily
verified that for every nonempty subset

S\subset[d]
,

\displaystyle\sup\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}0,t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $h$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\\
\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}>0\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{k\in S}\big(u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}-u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1}\big)\cdot\sup\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1},t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f-f^{*}$ }\\
u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1},&k\notin S&\\
\end{bmatrix}:u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1}<t_{k}<u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}<\infty

and

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},1,&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $h$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}<1\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{k\in S}\big(u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}-u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1}\big)\cdot\inf\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},u^{(k)}_{j_{k}},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f-f^{*}$ }\\
u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1},&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:u^{(k)}_{j_{k}-1}<t_{k}<u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}>-\infty.

By Proposition
2
, these facts ensure
that
h\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
. Furthermore, since

\|g-f^{*}\|_{2}^{2}=A_{\mathbf{j}}\cdot\|h\|_{2}^{2},

we obtain

\log N_{[\ ]}\big(\epsilon/\sqrt{m},\widebar{B}^{(\mathbf{j})}_{M}(t)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{2}\big)\leq\log N_{[\ ]}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{m^{1/2}A_{\mathbf{j}}^{1/2}},B_{M}\Big(\frac{t}{A_{\mathbf{j}}^{1/2}}\Big),\|\cdot\|_{2}\bigg),

where

B_{M}(t)=\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{TC}}^{d,s}:\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M\text{ and }\|f\|_{2}\leq t\}.

Using the bound (
73
) established in the special case
f^{*}=0
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}\big(\epsilon/\sqrt{m},\widebar{B}^{(\mathbf{j})}_{M}(t)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{2}\big)\leq C_{d,M}\log\Big(3+\frac{m^{1/2}t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{m^{1/2}t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{d+1/e}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C_{d,M}\log\Big(3+\frac{m^{1/2}t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{A_{\mathbf{j}}^{1/2}}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{d+1/e}+C_{d,M}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{m^{1/2}t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4+2(s-1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C_{d,M}\Big(3+\frac{m^{1/2}t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{m^{1/2}t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{2(s-1)}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{A_{\mathbf{j}}^{1/2}}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4+2(s-1)/e}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{d,M,m}\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{d+1/e}+C_{d,M,m}\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{d+1/e}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C_{d,M,m}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4+2(s-1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C_{d,M,m}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{2(s-1)}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4+2(s-1)/e},

where the second inequality also uses the fact that
A_{\mathbf{j}}\leq 1
.
Combining this bound with (
74
) and
(
75
) yields

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B_{M,p_{0}}(t)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq C_{b,B,d,M,m}\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{d+1/e}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C_{b,B,d,M,m}\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\cdot\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{d+1/e}+C_{b,B,d,M,m}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4+2(s-1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C_{b,B,d,M,m}\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{t}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)^{2(s-1)}\bigg(\log\Big(3+\frac{1}{t}\Big)\bigg)^{5d/4+2(s-1)/e}.

From here on, we can repeat the same argument and computations as in the case

f^{*}=0
. The only difference is that the constants now also depend on

m=m(f^{*})
.
As a result, we obtain

\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}=O_{p}\big(n^{-1/2}(\log n)^{a(d,s)}\big),

where the constants underlying
O_{p}
depend on
b,B,d,V,K,\sigma
, and

m(f^{*})
.
∎

C.4
Proofs of Lemmas in Appendix
C.1

C.4.1
Proof of Lemma
1

Our proof of Lemma
1
is based on the following lemma.
This lemma is a variant of
Aistleitner and Dick [1, Theorem 3]
, which we restate here as Theorem
12
.

Aistleitner and Dick [1, Theorem 3]
offers a connection between functions on
[0,1]^{m}
that are entirely monotone and coordinate-wise right-continuous, and the cumulative distribution functions of finite measures on
[0,1]^{m}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
.
The proof of Lemma
15
will be provided right after the proof of Lemma
1
.

Lemma 15
.

Suppose a real-valued function
g
on
[0,1]^{m}
is entirely monotone and coordinate-wise right-continuous.
Also, assume that
g
is coordinate-wise left-continuous at each point
\mathbf{x}\in[0,1]^{m}\setminus[0,1)^{m}
with respect to all the
j^{\text{th}}
coordinates where
x_{j}=1
.
Then, there exists a finite (Borel) measure
\nu
on
[0,1)^{m}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
such that

g(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})-g(0,\dots,0)=\nu\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{m}[0,x_{k}]\cap\big([0,1)^{m}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\big)\bigg)

(76)

for every
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\in[0,1]^{m}
.

Theorem 12

(Theorem 3 of
Aistleitner and Dick [1]
)
.

Suppose a real-valued function
g
on
[0,1]^{m}
is entirely monotone and coordinate-wise right-continuous.
Then, there exists a unique finite (Borel) measure
\nu
on
[0,1]^{m}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
such that

g(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})-g(0,\dots,0)=\nu\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{m}[0,x_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\bigg)

for every
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\in[0,1]^{m}
.

Proof of Lemma
1
.

First, we assume that
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
is of the form (
9
) and show that such
f
can be represented as (
34
).
For each
S\subseteq[d]
with
1\leq|S|\leq s
, let
g_{S}
be the function on
[0,1]^{|S|}
defined by

g_{S}((x_{k},k\in S))=-\beta_{S}+\nu_{S}\bigg(\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k}]\cap\big([0,1)^{m}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\big)\bigg)

for
(x_{k},k\in S)\in[0,1]^{|S|}
.
It can be readily checked that
g_{S}
is coordinate-wise right-continuous on
[0,1]^{|S|}
and coordinate-wise left-continuous at each point
(x_{k},k\in S)\in[0,1]^{|S|}\setminus[0,1)^{|S|}
with respect to all the
k^{\text{th}}
coordinates where
x_{k}=1
.
Moreover, for each
S
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}}\prod_{k\in S}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{S}(t_{k},k\in S)

\displaystyle=\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k})\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[t_{k},x_{k})}1\,d(s_{k},k\in S)\,d\nu_{S}(t_{k},k\in S)

\displaystyle=\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k})}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,s_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}}1\,d\nu_{S}(t_{k},k\in S)\,d(s_{k},k\in S)

\displaystyle=\beta_{S}\cdot\prod_{k\in S}x_{k}+\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k}]}g_{S}((s_{k},k\in S))\,d(s_{k},k\in S),

from which it follows that

\displaystyle f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}+\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}\cdot\prod_{k\in S}x_{k}-\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\int_{[0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}}\prod_{k\in S}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{S}(t_{k},k\in S)

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}-\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k}]}g_{S}((s_{k},k\in S))\,d(s_{k},k\in S).

Hence, it remains to verify that
g_{S}
is entirely monotone for each
S
.
Without loss of generality, we assume that
S=[m]
.
Recall that, when
S=[m]
,
g_{S}
is entirely monotone if

\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},\dots,x_{p_{1}+1}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&;\ g_{S}\\
x_{1}^{(m)},\dots,x_{p_{m}+1}^{(m)}&\end{bmatrix}\geq 0

for every
(p_{1},\dots,p_{m})\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
.
For each
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\dots,p_{m})
, this inequality follows from the nonnegativity of
\nu_{S}
and the following equation:

\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},\dots,x_{p_{1}+1}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&;\ g_{S}\\
x_{1}^{(m)},\dots,x_{p_{m}+1}^{(m)}&\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{\prod_{k\in S_{\mathbf{p}}}(x_{2}^{(k)}-x_{1}^{(k)})}\cdot\nu_{S}\bigg(\bigg(\prod_{k\in S_{\mathbf{p}}}\big(x_{1}^{(k)},x_{2}^{(k)}\big]\times\prod_{k\notin S_{\mathbf{p}}}\big[0,x_{1}^{(k)}\big]\bigg)\cap[0,1)^{m}\bigg),

where
S_{\mathbf{p}}=\{k\in\{1,\dots,m\}:p_{k}=1\}
.

Next, we assume that
f
is of the form (
34
) and show that
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
.
For each
S\subseteq[d]
with
1\leq|S|\leq s
, since
g_{S}
appearing in (
34
) satisfies the conditions of Lemma
15
, there exists a finite measure
\nu_{S}
on
[0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
such that

g_{S}((x_{k},k\in S))-g_{S}((0,k\in S))=\nu_{S}\bigg(\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k}]\cap\big([0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\big)\bigg)

for every
(x_{k},k\in S)\in[0,1]^{|S|}
.
If we let
\beta_{S}=-g_{S}((0,k\in S))
, then we can express
f
as

\displaystyle f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}+\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}\prod_{k\in S}x_{k}-\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k})}\nu_{S}\bigg(\prod_{k\in S}[0,t_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\bigg)\,d(t_{k},k\in S)

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}+\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}\prod_{k\in S}x_{k}-\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k})}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,t_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}}1\,d\nu_{S}(s_{k},k\in S)\,d(t_{k},k\in S)

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}+\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}\prod_{k\in S}x_{k}-\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[0,x_{k})\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}}\int_{\prod_{k\in S}[s_{k},x_{k})}1\,d(t_{k},k\in S)\,d\nu_{S}(s_{k},k\in S)

\displaystyle=\beta_{0}+\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}\prod_{k\in S}x_{k}-\sum_{S:1\leq|S|\leq s}\int_{[0,1)^{|S|}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}}\prod_{k\in S}(x_{k}-s_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{S}(s_{k},k\in S),

which proves that
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{TC}}}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
15
.

Since
g
satisfies the conditions of Theorem
12
, there exists a finite measure
\widebar{\nu}
on
[0,1]^{m}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
such that

g(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})-g(0,\dots,0)=\widebar{\nu}\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{m}[0,x_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\bigg)

for every
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\in[0,1]^{m}
.
Let
\nu
be the measure obtained by restricting
\widebar{\nu}
to
[0,1)^{m}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
.
We show that
\nu
is a desired measure, i.e.,
\nu
satisfies the equation (
76
) for every
\mathbf{x}\in[0,1]^{m}
.
For
\mathbf{x}\in[0,1)^{m}
, (
76
) is clear from the fact that

\widebar{\nu}\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{m}[0,x_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\bigg)=\widebar{\nu}\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{m}[0,x_{k}]\cap\big([0,1)^{m}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\big)\bigg).

Hence, we only need to prove (
76
) for
\mathbf{x}\in[0,1]^{m}\setminus[0,1)^{m}
.
Without loss of generality, let
\mathbf{x}=(1,\dots,1,x_{l+1},\dots,x_{m})
where
x_{l+1},\dots,x_{m}<1
.
Then, using the coordinate-wise left-continuity of
g
at
\mathbf{x}
, we can show that

\displaystyle g(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})-g(0,\dots,0)=\lim_{y_{1}\uparrow 1}\dots\lim_{y_{l}\uparrow 1}g(y_{1},\dots,y_{l},x_{l+1},\dots,x_{m})-g(0,\dots,0)

\displaystyle\qquad=\lim_{y_{1}\uparrow 1}\dots\lim_{y_{l}\uparrow 1}\widebar{\nu}\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{l}[0,y_{k}]\times\prod_{k=l+1}^{m}[0,x_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\bigg)=\widebar{\nu}\bigg([0,1)^{l}\times\prod_{k=l+1}^{m}[0,x_{k}]\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad=\widebar{\nu}\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{m}[0,x_{k}]\cap\big([0,1)^{m}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\big)\bigg)=\nu\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{m}[0,x_{k}]\cap\big([0,1)^{m}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}\big)\bigg),

as desired.
∎

C.4.2
Proof of Lemma
2

Proof of Lemma
2
.

We prove that the divided difference of
f
of order
\mathbf{p}
is nonpositive on
[0,1]^{d}
for every
\mathbf{p}\in\{0,1,2\}^{d}
with
\max_{k}p_{k}=2
by induction on
|\mathbf{p}|:=|\{k:p_{k}\neq 0\}|
.
It is clear that the claim is true when
|\mathbf{p}|=d
, since
N^{(\mathbf{p})}=[0,1]^{d}
in this case.
Suppose that the claim is true when
|\mathbf{p}|=m+1
for some
m<d
and fix a nonnegative integer vector
\mathbf{p}\in\{0,1,2\}^{d}
with
\max_{k}p_{k}=2
and
|\mathbf{p}|=m
.
Without loss of generality, we assume that
\{k:p_{k}\neq 0\}=[m]
.
Then, for every
0\leq x_{1}^{(k)}<\cdots<x_{p_{k}+1}^{(k)}\leq 1
,
k=1,\dots,d
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},\dots,x_{p_{1}+1}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(m)},\dots,x_{p_{m}+1}^{(m)}&\\
x_{1}^{(m+1)}&;\ f\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d-1)}&\\
x_{1}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=x_{1}^{(d)}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},\dots,x_{p_{1}+1}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(m)},\dots,x_{p_{m}+1}^{(m)}&\\
x_{1}^{(m+1)}&;\ f\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d-1)}&\\
x_{1}^{(d)},0&\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},\dots,x_{p_{1}+1}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(m)},\dots,x_{p_{m}+1}^{(m)}&\\
x_{1}^{(m+1)}&;\ f\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d-1)}&\\
0&\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},\dots,x_{p_{1}+1}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(m)},\dots,x_{p_{m}+1}^{(m)}&\\
x_{1}^{(m+1)}&;\ f\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d-1)}&\\
0&\end{bmatrix},

where the inequality is due to the fact that the divided difference of
f
of order
(p_{1},\dots,p_{m},0,\dots,0,1)
is nonpositive because
|(p_{1},\dots,p_{m},0,\dots,0,1)|=m+1
.
If we repeat this argument with the induction hypothesis that the divided differences of
f
of order
(p_{1},\dots,p_{m},0,\dots,0,1,0),\dots,(p_{1},\dots,p_{m},1,0,\dots,0)
are nonpositive, we can show that

\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},\dots,x_{p_{1}+1}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(m)},\dots,x_{p_{m}+1}^{(m)}&\\
x_{1}^{(m+1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\leq\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},\dots,x_{p_{1}+1}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(m)},\dots,x_{p_{m}+1}^{(m)}&\\
0&\\
\vdots&\\
0&\end{bmatrix}.

Since the divided difference of
f
of order
(p_{1},\dots,p_{m},0,\dots,0)
is nonpositive on
N^{(p_{1},\dots,p_{m},0,\dots,0)}=[0,1]^{m}\times\{0\}\times\cdots\times\{0\}
by the assumption, the right-hand side of this inequality is nonpositive.
This proves that the divided difference of
f
of order
\mathbf{p}
is nonpositive on
[0,1]^{d}
and hence concludes the proof.
∎

C.4.3
Proof of Lemma
3

Proof of Lemma
3
.

The last part is a direct consequence of the first two.
This is because

\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},x_{2}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&;\ f\\
x_{1}^{(d)},x_{2}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\geq\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},y_{2}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&;\ f\\
x_{1}^{(d)},y_{2}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\geq\begin{bmatrix}y_{1}^{(1)},y_{2}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&;\ f\\
y_{1}^{(d)},y_{2}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}

since
x_{1}^{(k)}\leq y_{1}^{(k)}
and
x_{2}^{(k)}\leq y_{2}^{(k)}
for
k=1,\dots,d
.

Suppose we are given
x_{1}^{(k)}<x_{2}^{(k)}\leq x_{3}^{(k)}
for
k=1,\dots,d
.
Since the divided difference of
f
of order
(1,\dots,1,2)
is nonpositive, we have the inequality

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},x_{2}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d-1)},x_{2}^{(d-1)}&\\
x_{1}^{(d)},x_{2}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=-\big(x_{3}^{(d)}-x_{2}^{(d)}\big)\cdot\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},x_{2}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d-1)},x_{2}^{(d-1)}&\\
x_{1}^{(d)},x_{2}^{(d)},x_{3}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},x_{2}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d-1)},x_{2}^{(d-1)}&\\
x_{1}^{(d)},x_{3}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\geq\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},x_{2}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d-1)},x_{2}^{(d-1)}&\\
x_{1}^{(d)},x_{3}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}.

Applying the same argument to each coordinate in turn and leveraging the assumption that the divided differences of
f
of order
(1,\dots,1,2,1),\dots,(2,1,\dots,1)
are nonpositive,
we can derive that

\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},x_{2}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d-1)},x_{2}^{(d-1)}&\\
x_{1}^{(d)},x_{2}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\geq\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},x_{2}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d-1)},x_{2}^{(d-1)}&\\
x_{1}^{(d)},x_{3}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\geq\cdots\geq\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},x_{3}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d-1)},x_{3}^{(d-1)}&\\
x_{1}^{(d)},x_{3}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix},

as desired.
By the same argument, we can show that

\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},x_{3}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&;\ f\\
x_{1}^{(d)},x_{3}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\geq\begin{bmatrix}x_{2}^{(1)},x_{3}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&;\ f\\
x_{2}^{(d)},x_{3}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}

when we are given
x_{1}^{(k)}\leq x_{2}^{(k)}<x_{3}^{(k)}
for
k=1,\dots,d
.
∎

C.4.4
Proof of Lemma
4

Proof of Lemma
4
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\mathbf{p}=(1,\dots,1,0,\dots,0)
and
m=|\{k:p_{k}\neq 0\}|
.
Fix a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
and, for each
k=1,\dots,d
, let
y_{1}^{(k)}=z_{1}^{(k)}=x_{1}^{(k)}
,
y_{2}^{(k)}=z_{2}^{(k)}=x_{1}^{(k)}+\epsilon
and, if
p_{k}=1
, further let
z_{3}^{(k)}=x_{2}^{(k)}-\epsilon
and
y_{3}^{(k)}=z_{4}^{(k)}=x_{2}^{(k)}
.
Since the divided difference of
f
of order
(p_{1}+1,\dots,p_{d}+1)=(2,\dots,2,1,\dots,1)
is nonpositive, we have the inequality

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geq\begin{bmatrix}y_{1}^{(1)},y_{2}^{(1)},y_{3}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
y_{1}^{(m)},y_{2}^{(m)},y_{3}^{(m)}&\\
y_{1}^{(m+1)},y_{2}^{(m+1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
y_{1}^{(d)},y_{2}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{3}\cdots\sum_{i_{m}=1}^{3}\sum_{i_{m+1}=1}^{2}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{2}\frac{1}{\prod_{j_{1}\neq i_{1}}(y_{i_{1}}^{(1)}-y_{j_{1}}^{(1)})}\times\cdots\times\frac{1}{\prod_{j_{m}\neq i_{m}}(y_{i_{m}}^{(m)}-y_{j_{m}}^{(m)})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon^{d-m}}\cdot(-1)^{\sum_{k=m+1}^{d}i_{k}}\cdot f\big(y_{i_{1}}^{(1)},\dots,y_{i_{d}}^{(d)}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}(y_{3}^{(k)}-y_{1}^{(k)})}\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon^{d-m}}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}\delta_{k}}\cdot\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}(y_{3-\delta_{k}}^{(k)}-y_{2-\delta_{k}}^{(k)})}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=2-\delta_{1}}^{3-\delta_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{m}=2-\delta_{m}}^{3-\delta_{m}}\sum_{i_{m+1}=1}^{2}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{2}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}(i_{k}+\delta_{k}-1)}\cdot(-1)^{\sum_{k=m+1}^{d}i_{k}}\cdot f\big(y_{i_{1}}^{(1)},\dots,y_{i_{d}}^{(d)}\big)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}(y_{3}^{(k)}-y_{1}^{(k)})}\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon^{d-m}}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}\delta_{k}}\cdot\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}(y_{3-\delta_{k}}^{(k)}-y_{2-\delta_{k}}^{(k)})}\sum_{i_{1}=2-\delta_{1}}^{3-\delta_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{m}=2-\delta_{m}}^{3-\delta_{m}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\sum_{i_{m+1}=1}^{2}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{2}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}(i_{k}+\delta_{k}-1)}\cdot(-1)^{\sum_{k=m+1}^{d}i_{k}}\cdot f\big(y_{i_{1}}^{(1)},\dots,y_{i_{m}}^{(m)},z_{i_{m+1}}^{(m+1)},\dots,z_{i_{d}}^{(d)}\big).\end{split}

(77)

Also, we have

\begin{split}\eqref{eq:first-order-ineq-first}&\geq\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}(y_{3}^{(k)}-y_{1}^{(k)})}\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon^{d-m}}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}\delta_{k}}\cdot\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m-1}(y_{3-\delta_{k}}^{(k)}-y_{2-\delta_{k}}^{(k)})}\cdot\frac{1}{z_{4-2\delta_{m}}^{(m)}-z_{3-2\delta_{m}}^{(m)}}\\
&\qquad\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=2-\delta_{1}}^{3-\delta_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{m-1}=2-\delta_{m-1}}^{3-\delta_{m-1}}\sum_{i_{m}=3-2\delta_{m}}^{4-2\delta_{m}}\sum_{i_{m+1}=1}^{2}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}(i_{k}+\delta_{k}-1)}\cdot(-1)^{\sum_{k=m}^{d}i_{k}}\cdot f\big(y_{i_{1}}^{(1)},\dots,y_{i_{m-1}}^{(m-1)},z_{i_{m}}^{(m)},\dots,z_{i_{d}}^{(d)}\big),\end{split}

(78)

which is because

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:first-order-ineq-second}-\eqref{eq:first-order-ineq-first}

\displaystyle=\frac{z_{3}^{(m)}-z_{2}^{(m)}}{y_{3}^{(m)}-y_{1}^{(m)}}\cdot\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m-1}(y_{3}^{(k)}-y_{1}^{(k)})}\cdot\frac{1}{z_{4}^{(m)}-z_{2}^{(m)}}\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon^{d-m}}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}\delta_{k}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m-1}(y_{3-\delta_{k}}^{(k)}-y_{2-\delta_{k}}^{(k)})}\cdot\frac{1}{z_{4-\delta_{m}}^{(m)}-z_{3-\delta_{m}}^{(m)}}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=2-\delta_{1}}^{3-\delta_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{m-1}=2-\delta_{m-1}}^{3-\delta_{m-1}}\sum_{i_{m}=3-\delta_{m}}^{4-\delta_{m}}\sum_{i_{m+1}=1}^{2}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}(i_{k}+\delta_{k}-1)}\cdot(-1)^{i_{m}+\delta_{m}}\cdot(-1)^{\sum_{k=m+1}^{d}i_{k}}\cdot f\big(y_{i_{1}}^{(1)},\dots,y_{i_{m-1}}^{(m-1)},z_{i_{m}}^{(m)},\dots,z_{i_{d}}^{(d)}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{z_{3}^{(m)}-z_{2}^{(m)}}{y_{3}^{(m)}-y_{1}^{(m)}}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}y_{1}^{(1)},y_{2}^{(1)},y_{3}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
y_{1}^{(m-1)},y_{2}^{(m-1)},y_{3}^{(m-1)}&\\
z_{2}^{(m)},z_{3}^{(m)},z_{4}^{(m)}&;\ f\\
y_{1}^{(m+1)},y_{2}^{(m+1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
y_{1}^{(d)},y_{2}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\leq 0.

Since
z_{4}^{(m)}-z_{3}^{(m)}=z_{2}^{(m)}-z_{1}^{(m)}=\epsilon
, it follows that

\displaystyle 0\geq\eqref{eq:first-order-ineq-second}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}(y_{3}^{(k)}-y_{1}^{(k)})}\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon^{d-m+1}}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}\delta_{k}}\cdot\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m-1}(y_{3-\delta_{k}}^{(k)}-y_{2-\delta_{k}}^{(k)})}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=2-\delta_{1}}^{3-\delta_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{m-1}=2-\delta_{m-1}}^{3-\delta_{m-1}}\sum_{i_{m}=3-2\delta_{m}}^{4-2\delta_{m}}\sum_{i_{m+1}=1}^{2}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}(i_{k}+\delta_{k}-1)}\cdot(-1)^{\sum_{k=m}^{d}i_{k}}\cdot f\big(y_{i_{1}}^{(1)},\dots,y_{i_{m-1}}^{(m-1)},z_{i_{m}}^{(m)},\dots,z_{i_{d}}^{(d)}\big).

If we apply the same argument to the
(m-1)^{\text{th}},\dots,1^{\text{st}}
coordinates sequentially, we obtain that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}(y_{3}^{(k)}-y_{1}^{(k)})}\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon^{d}}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}\delta_{k}}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=3-2\delta_{1}}^{4-2\delta_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{m}=3-2\delta_{m}}^{4-2\delta_{m}}\sum_{i_{m+1}=1}^{2}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{2}f\big(z_{i_{1}}^{(1)},\dots,z_{i_{d}}^{(d)}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}(x_{2}^{(k)}-x_{1}^{(k)})}\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon^{d}}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}\delta_{k}}\triangle f\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big[z_{3-2\delta_{k}}^{(k)},z_{4-2\delta_{k}}^{(k)}\big]\times\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}\big[z_{1}^{(k)},z_{2}^{(k)}\big]\bigg).

If we take the limit
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, this gives that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}(x_{2}^{(k)}-x_{1}^{(k)})}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{2}\cdots\sum_{i_{m}=1}^{2}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}i_{k}}D\triangle f\big(x_{i_{1}}^{(1)},\dots,x_{i_{m}}^{(m)},x_{1}^{(m+1)},\dots,x_{1}^{(d)}\big)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}x_{1}^{(1)},x_{2}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $D\triangle f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(m)},x_{2}^{(m)}&\\
x_{1}^{(m+1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
x_{1}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix},

as desired.
∎

C.5
Proofs of Lemmas in Appendix
C.3

C.5.1
Proof of Lemma
11

Proof of Lemma
11
.

The following lemma will be repeatedly used in our proof of Lemma
11
.
This lemma can be viewed as a generalization of Lemma
3
, and it can be proved similarly to Lemma
3
.

Lemma 16
.

Suppose
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d}_{\text{TCP}}}
.
Then, for every nonempty subset
S\subseteq[d]
, we have

\begin{bmatrix}v_{1}^{(k)},v_{2}^{(k)},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
v_{1}^{(k)},&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}\geq\begin{bmatrix}w_{1}^{(k)},w_{2}^{(k)},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
w_{1}^{(k)},&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}

provided that
0\leq v_{1}^{(k)}\leq w_{1}^{(k)}\leq 1
and
0\leq v_{2}^{(k)}\leq w_{2}^{(k)}\leq 1
for
k\in S
, and
0\leq v_{1}^{(k)}=w_{1}^{(k)}\leq 1
for
k\notin S
.

Also, note that since
g^{*}
satisfies the interaction restriction condition (
13
) (recall (
32
)) for every subset
S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|>s
, in the definition (
55
) of
M(g^{*})
, we can equivalently take the maximum over all nonempty subsets
S\subseteq[d]
as follows:

\begin{split}M=M(g^{*})=\max_{\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[d]}\max\bigg(&\sup\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}0,t_{k},&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}>0\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\},\\
&\quad-\inf\bigg\{\begin{bmatrix}t_{k},1,&k\in S&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
0,&k\notin S&\end{bmatrix}:t_{k}<1\ \text{ for }k\in S\bigg\}\bigg).\end{split}

(79)

Before proving (
56
) and (
57
), we show that
there exists some constant
B_{d}
depending on
d
such that

\bigg|\begin{bmatrix}v_{1}^{(1)},\dots,v_{p_{1}+1}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&;g^{*}\\
v_{1}^{(d)},\dots,v_{p_{d}+1}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\bigg|\leq B_{d}M

(80)

for every
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\dots,p_{d})\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
and
0\leq v_{1}^{(k)}<\dots<v_{p_{k}+1}^{(k)}\leq 1
for
k=1,\dots,d
.
Since
\theta^{*}=(g^{*}(\mathbf{x}^{(\mathbf{i})}),\mathbf{i}\in I_{0})
, this result directly implies that

\bigg|\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{p_{k}}\bigg)\cdot(D^{(\mathbf{p})}\theta^{*})_{\mathbf{i}}\bigg|\leq B_{d}M

(81)

for every
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\dots,p_{d})\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
and
\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})\in I_{0}
with
i_{k}\geq p_{k}
for
k=1,\dots,d
.

We prove (
80
) by induction on
|\mathbf{p}|:=|\{k:p_{k}\neq 0\}|
.
When
|\mathbf{p}|=d
(
\mathbf{p}=(1,\dots,1)
), it directly follows from Lemma
16
and (
79
) that the inequality (
80
) holds with
B_{d}=1=:B_{d,d}
.
Suppose that the inequality (
80
) holds with
B_{d}=B_{d,m+1}
for every
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\dots,p_{d})\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{p}|=m+1
, and fix
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\dots,p_{d})\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{p}|=m
.
Without loss of generality, let
\mathbf{p}=(1,\dots,1,0,\dots,0)
.
Observe that

\displaystyle\bigg|\begin{bmatrix}v_{1}^{(1)},v_{2}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
\vdots&\\
v_{1}^{(m)},v_{2}^{(m)}&\\
v_{1}^{(m+1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
v_{1}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\begin{bmatrix}v_{1}^{(1)},v_{2}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
v_{1}^{(m)},v_{2}^{(m)}&\\
0&;g^{*}\\
v_{1}^{(m+2)}&\\
\vdots&\\
v_{1}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\bigg|+v_{1}^{(m+1)}\cdot\bigg|\begin{bmatrix}v_{1}^{(1)},v_{2}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
v_{1}^{(m)},v_{2}^{(m)}&\\
0,v_{1}^{(m+1)}&;g^{*}\\
v_{1}^{(m+2)}&\\
\vdots&\\
v_{1}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\begin{bmatrix}v_{1}^{(1)},v_{2}^{(1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
v_{1}^{(m)},v_{2}^{(m)}&\\
0&;g^{*}\\
v_{1}^{(m+2)}&\\
\vdots&\\
v_{1}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\bigg|+B_{d,m+1}M.

Repeating this argument with the
(m+2)^{\text{th}},\dots,d^{\text{th}}
coordinates, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\begin{bmatrix}v_{1}^{(1)},v_{2}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
\vdots&\\
v_{1}^{(m)},v_{2}^{(m)}&\\
v_{1}^{(m+1)}&\\
\vdots&\\
v_{1}^{(d)}&\end{bmatrix}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\begin{bmatrix}v_{1}^{(1)},v_{2}^{(1)}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $g^{*}$ }\\
\vdots&\\
v_{1}^{(m)},v_{2}^{(m)}&\\
0&\\
\vdots&\\
0&\end{bmatrix}\bigg|+(d-m)\cdot B_{d,m+1}M

\displaystyle\leq M+(d-m)\cdot B_{d,m+1}M=\big(1+(d-m)\cdot B_{d,m+1}\big)M,

where the second inequality is again from Lemma
16
and (
79
).
This proves that the inequality (
80
) holds with
B_{d}=1+(d-m)\cdot B_{d,m+1}=:B_{d,m}
for every
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\dots,p_{d})\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{p}|=m
.
Consequently, the inequality (
80
) holds for every
\mathbf{p}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
, if
B_{d}=\max_{m=1,\dots,d}B_{d,m}
.

Now, we are ready to prove (
56
) and (
57
).
We also prove them by induction on
|\mathbf{p}|
.
Let us first consider the case
|\mathbf{p}|=d
(
\mathbf{p}=(1,\dots,1)
).
Suppose, for contradiction, that there exist
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}\{1,\dots,R_{k}\}
and
\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})\in L_{\mathbf{r}}
such that

(D^{(\mathbf{p})}\theta)_{\mathbf{i}}<\frac{C_{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}}\cdot\Big[-M-t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}\Big],

where
C_{d}=\max((2\sqrt{3})^{d},B_{d})=:C^{L}_{d,d}
.
Then, by the definition (
50
) of discrete differences and Lemma
