16
, for every
\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})\in I_{0}
with
j_{k}\geq i_{k}
for
k=1,\dots,d
, we have

\displaystyle(D^{(\mathbf{p})}\theta)_{\mathbf{j}}

\displaystyle\leq(D^{(\mathbf{p})}\theta)_{\mathbf{i}}<\frac{C_{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}}\cdot\Big[-M-t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq(D^{(\mathbf{p})}\theta^{*})_{\mathbf{j}}-\frac{C_{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}}\cdot t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}.

Here, for the last inequality, we use (
81
) and the fact that
C_{d}\geq B_{d}
.
It thus follows that if
i_{1}-1\leq j^{\prime}_{1}<j_{1},\dots,i_{d}-1\leq j^{\prime}_{d}<j_{d}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}(\theta-\theta^{*})^{2}_{(1-\delta_{1})j_{1}+\delta_{1}j^{\prime}_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{d})j_{d}+\delta_{d}j^{\prime}_{d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{1}{2^{d}}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}(-1)^{\delta_{1}+\cdots+\delta_{d}}\cdot(\theta-\theta^{*})_{(1-\delta_{1})j_{1}+\delta_{1}j^{\prime}_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{d})j_{d}+\delta_{d}j^{\prime}_{d}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{2^{d}}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{l_{1}=j^{\prime}_{1}+1}^{j_{1}}\cdots\sum_{l_{d}=j^{\prime}_{d}+1}^{j_{d}}\big(D^{(\mathbf{p})}\theta-D^{(\mathbf{p})}\theta^{*}\big)_{l_{1},\dots,l_{d}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad>(j_{1}-j^{\prime}_{1})^{2}\times\cdots\times(j_{d}-j^{\prime}_{d})^{2}\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}/2^{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}2r_{k}}.

This implies that

\displaystyle t^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|\theta-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\sum_{(i_{1}+n_{1}-2)/2<j_{1}\leq n_{1}-1}\cdots\sum_{(i_{d}+n_{d}-2)/2<j_{d}\leq n_{d}-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}(\theta-\theta^{*})^{2}_{(1-\delta_{1})j_{1}+\delta_{1}(i_{1}+n_{1}-2-j_{1}),\dots,(1-\delta_{d})j_{d}+\delta_{d}(i_{d}+n_{d}-2-j_{d})}

\displaystyle>\sum_{(i_{1}+n_{1}-2)/2<j_{1}\leq n_{1}-1}\cdots\sum_{(i_{d}+n_{d}-2)/2<j_{d}\leq n_{d}-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad(2j_{1}-i_{1}-n_{1}+2)^{2}\times\cdots\times(2j_{d}-i_{d}-n_{d}+2)^{2}\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}/2^{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{d}\Big[\frac{1}{6}(n_{k}-i_{k})(n_{k}-i_{k}+1)(n_{k}-i_{k}+2)\Big]\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}/2^{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle>\prod_{k=1}^{d}(n_{k}-i_{k})^{3}\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}/12^{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle>\prod_{k=1}^{d}\Big(\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}}}\Big)^{3}\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}/12^{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}2r_{k}}=\frac{C_{d}^{2}}{12^{d}}\cdot t^{2}\geq t^{2},

which leads to contradiction.
Here, the second-to-last inequality is from the fact that, for each
k
,

n_{k}-i_{k}\geq n_{k}-\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}}}+1>\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}}},

since
r_{k}\geq 1
.

Next, suppose, for contradiction, that there exist
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}\{0,1,\dots,R_{k}\}
and
\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})\in L_{\mathbf{r}}
with
i_{k}\geq 1=p_{k}
for
k=1,\dots,d
such that

(D^{(\mathbf{p})}\theta)_{\mathbf{i}}>\frac{C_{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}}\cdot\Big[M+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}\Big],

where
C_{d}=\max((8\sqrt{2})^{d},B_{d})=:C^{U}_{d,d}
.
Then, for every
\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})\in I_{0}
with
j_{k}\leq i_{k}
, we have

(D^{(\mathbf{p})}\theta)_{\mathbf{j}}\geq(D^{(\mathbf{p})}\theta)_{\mathbf{i}}>\frac{C_{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}}\cdot\Big[M+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}\Big]\geq(D^{(\mathbf{p})}\theta^{*})_{\mathbf{j}}+\frac{C_{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}}\cdot t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}.

By the same argument as above, we can show that

\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}(\theta-\theta^{*})^{2}_{(1-\delta_{1})j_{1}+\delta_{1}j^{\prime}_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{d})j_{d}+\delta_{d}j^{\prime}_{d}}>(j_{1}-j^{\prime}_{1})^{2}\times\cdots\times(j_{d}-j^{\prime}_{d})^{2}\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}/2^{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}2r_{k}}

provided that
0\leq j^{\prime}_{1}<j_{1}\leq i_{1},\dots,0\leq j^{\prime}_{d}<j_{d}\leq i_{d}
.
It thus follows that

\displaystyle t^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|\theta-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\sum_{i_{1}/2<j_{1}\leq i_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}/2<j_{d}\leq i_{d}}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}(\theta-\theta^{*})^{2}_{(1-\delta_{1})j_{1}+\delta_{1}(i_{1}-j_{1}),\dots,(1-\delta_{d})j_{d}+\delta_{d}(i_{d}-j_{d})}

\displaystyle>\sum_{i_{1}/2<j_{1}\leq i_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}/2<j_{d}\leq i_{d}}(2j_{1}-i_{1})^{2}\times\cdots\times(2j_{d}-i_{d})^{2}\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}/2^{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{d}\Big[\frac{1}{6}i_{k}(i_{k}+1)(i_{k}+2)\Big]\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}/2^{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{k=1}^{d}(i_{k}+1)^{3}\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}/16^{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle>\prod_{k=1}^{d}\Big(\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}+1}}\Big)^{3}\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}/16^{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}2r_{k}}=\frac{C_{d}^{2}}{128^{d}}\cdot t^{2}\geq t^{2},

which leads to contradiction.
Here, for the fourth inequality, we use

\frac{4}{3}i_{k}(i_{k}+1)(i_{k}+2)\geq(i_{k}+1)^{3},

which holds since
i_{k}\geq 1
.

Now, we assume that (
56
) and (
57
) hold with
C_{d}=C^{L}_{d,m+1}
and
C_{d}=C^{U}_{d,m+1}
, respectively, for every
\mathbf{p}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{p}|=m+1
,
and prove that they also hold with some
C_{d}=C^{L}_{d,m}
and
C_{d}=C^{U}_{d,m}
for every
\mathbf{p}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{p}|=m
.
For notational convenience, here, we only consider the case where
\mathbf{p}=(1,\dots,1,0,\dots,0):=\mathbf{1}_{m}
, but the same argument applies to other
\mathbf{p}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{p}|=m
.

Suppose, for contradiction, that there exist
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}\{1,\dots,R_{k}\}
and
\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})\in L_{\mathbf{r}}
such that

(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{\mathbf{i}}<\frac{C_{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot\Big[-M-t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\Big],

where

C_{d}=\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}+\max((2\sqrt{3})^{m},B_{d})=:C^{L}_{d,m}.

Then, by the induction hypothesis,
for
\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})\in I_{0}
with
j_{1}\geq i_{1},\dots,j_{m}\geq i_{m}
, and
i_{m+1}\leq j_{m+1}\leq b^{(m+1)}_{r_{m+1}-1},\dots,i_{d}\leq j_{d}\leq b^{(d)}_{r_{d}-1}
, we have

\displaystyle(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}}

\displaystyle=(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{j_{1},\dots,j_{m},i_{m+1},j_{m+2},\dots,j_{d}}+\sum_{l_{m+1}=i_{m+1}+1}^{j_{m+1}}(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m+1})}\theta)_{j_{1},\dots,j_{m},l_{m+1},j_{m+2},\dots,j_{d}}

\displaystyle\leq(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{j_{1},\dots,j_{m},i_{m+1},j_{m+2},\dots,j_{d}}+(j_{m+1}-i_{m+1})\cdot\frac{C^{U}_{d,m+1}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m+1}n_{k}}\cdot\Big[M+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}r_{k}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{j_{1},\dots,j_{m},i_{m+1},j_{m+2},\dots,j_{d}}+\frac{3}{2}\cdot\frac{C^{U}_{d,m+1}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot\bigg[\frac{M}{2^{r_{m+1}}}+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\bigg],

where
\mathbf{1}_{m+1}=(1,\dots,1,0,\dots,0)
with
|\mathbf{1}_{m+1}|=m+1
.
Here, the second inequality is because

j_{m+1}-i_{m+1}\leq b^{(m+1)}_{r_{m+1}-1}-i_{m+1}<\frac{n_{m+1}}{2^{r_{m+1}-1}}-1-\Big(\frac{n_{m+1}}{2^{r_{m+1}+1}}-1\Big)=\frac{3}{2}\cdot\frac{n_{m+1}}{2^{r_{m+1}}}.

Repeating this argument with the
(m+2)^{\text{th}},\dots,d^{\text{th}}
coordinates in turn, we can derive that

\displaystyle(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{j_{1},\dots,j_{m},i_{m+1},\dots,i_{d}}+\frac{3}{2}\cdot\frac{C^{U}_{d,m+1}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=m+1}^{d}\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}}}\bigg)\cdot M+(d-m)\cdot t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{j_{1},\dots,j_{m},i_{m+1},\dots,i_{d}}+\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)\cdot\frac{C^{U}_{d,m+1}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot\bigg[M+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{i_{1},\dots,i_{m},i_{m+1},\dots,i_{d}}+\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)\cdot\frac{C^{U}_{d,m+1}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot\bigg[M+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad<-\Big(C_{d}-\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot\bigg[M+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta^{*})_{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}}-\Big(C_{d}-\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}},

where the third inequality follows from Lemma
16
and the definition (
50
) of discrete differences, and the last inequality is due to (
81
) and the fact that

\widetilde{C}_{d}:=C_{d}-\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}\geq B_{d}.

It thus follows that if
i_{1}-1\leq j^{\prime}_{1}<j_{1},\dots,i_{m}-1\leq j^{\prime}_{m}<j_{m}
, and if
i_{m+1}\leq j_{m+1}\leq b^{(m+1)}_{r_{m+1}-1},\dots,i_{d}\leq j_{d}\leq b^{(d)}_{r_{d}-1}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(\theta-\theta^{*})^{2}_{(1-\delta_{1})j_{1}+\delta_{1}j^{\prime}_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{m})j_{m}+\delta_{m}j^{\prime}_{m},j_{m+1},\dots,j_{d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{1}{2^{m}}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(-1)^{\delta_{1}+\cdots+\delta_{m}}\cdot(\theta-\theta^{*})_{(1-\delta_{1})j_{1}+\delta_{1}j^{\prime}_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{m})j_{m}+\delta_{m}j^{\prime}_{m},j_{m+1},\dots,j_{d}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{2^{m}}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{l_{1}=j^{\prime}_{1}+1}^{j_{1}}\cdots\sum_{l_{m}=j^{\prime}_{m}+1}^{j_{m}}\big(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta-D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta^{*}\big)_{l_{1},\dots,l_{m},j_{m+1},\dots,j_{d}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad>(j_{1}-j^{\prime}_{1})^{2}\times\cdots\times(j_{m}-j^{\prime}_{m})^{2}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}/2^{m}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}2r_{k}}.

This implies that

\displaystyle t^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|\theta-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\sum_{(i_{1}+n_{1}-2)/2<j_{1}\leq n_{1}-1}\cdots\sum_{(i_{m}+n_{m}-2)/2<j_{m}\leq n_{m}-1}\sum_{j_{m+1}=i_{m+1}}^{b^{(m+1)}_{r_{m+1}-1}}\cdots\sum_{j_{d}=i_{d}}^{b^{(d)}_{r_{d}-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(\theta-\theta^{*})^{2}_{(1-\delta_{1})j_{1}+\delta_{1}(i_{1}+n_{1}-2-j_{1}),\dots,(1-\delta_{m})j_{m}+\delta_{m}(i_{m}+n_{m}-2-j_{m}),j_{m+1},\dots,j_{d}}

\displaystyle>\sum_{(i_{1}+n_{1}-2)/2<j_{1}\leq n_{1}-1}\cdots\sum_{(i_{m}+n_{m}-2)/2<j_{m}\leq n_{m}-1}\sum_{j_{m+1}=i_{m+1}}^{b^{(m+1)}_{r_{m+1}-1}}\cdots\sum_{j_{d}=i_{d}}^{b^{(d)}_{r_{d}-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad(2j_{1}-i_{1}-n_{1}+2)^{2}\times\cdots\times(2j_{m}-i_{m}-n_{m}+2)^{2}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}/2^{m}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{m}\Big[\frac{1}{6}(n_{k}-i_{k})(n_{k}-i_{k}+1)(n_{k}-i_{k}+2)\Big]\cdot\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}\big(b^{(k)}_{r_{k}-1}-i_{k}+1\big)\cdot\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}/2^{m}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle>\prod_{k=1}^{m}(n_{k}-i_{k})^{3}\cdot\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}\big(b^{(k)}_{r_{k}-1}-i_{k}+1\big)\cdot\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}/12^{m}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle>\prod_{k=1}^{m}\Big(\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}}}\Big)^{3}\cdot\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}}}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}/12^{m}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}2r_{k}}=\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}}{12^{m}}\cdot t^{2}\geq t^{2},

which leads to contradiction.
Here, the second-to-last inequality is because

b^{(k)}_{r_{k}-1}-i_{k}+1>\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}-1}}-2-\Big(\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}}}-1\Big)+1=\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}}}

for each
k=m+1,\dots,d
.

Next, suppose, for contradiction, that there exist
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\prod_{k=1}^{d}\{0,1,\dots,R_{k}\}
and
\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})\in L_{\mathbf{r}}
with
i_{k}\geq 1=p_{k}
for
k=1,\dots,d
such that

(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{\mathbf{i}}>\frac{C_{d}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot\Big[M+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\Big],

where

C_{d}=(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}+\max((4\sqrt{2})^{m}\cdot 2^{d},B_{d})=:C^{U}_{d,m}.

It then follows that, for every
\mathbf{j}=(j_{1},\dots,j_{d})\in I_{0}
with
j_{1}\leq i_{1},\dots,j_{m}\leq i_{m}
, and
a^{(m+1)}_{r_{m+1}+1}\leq j_{m+1}\leq i_{m+1},\dots,a^{(d)}_{r_{d}+1}\leq j_{d}\leq i_{d}
(if
r_{k}=R_{k}
, let
a^{(k)}_{R_{k}+1}=0
),

\displaystyle(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}}

\displaystyle\geq(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{i_{1},\dots,i_{m},j_{m+1},\dots,j_{d}}

\displaystyle=(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{i_{1},\dots,i_{m},i_{m+1},j_{m+2},\dots,j_{d}}-\sum_{l_{m+1}=j_{m+1}+1}^{i_{m+1}}(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m+1})}\theta)_{i_{1},\dots,i_{m},l_{m+1},j_{m+2},\dots,j_{d}}

\displaystyle\geq(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{i_{1},\dots,i_{m},i_{m+1},j_{m+2},\dots,j_{d}}-(i_{m+1}-j_{m+1})\cdot\frac{C^{U}_{d,m+1}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m+1}n_{k}}\cdot\Big[M+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}r_{k}}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{i_{1},\dots,i_{m},i_{m+1},j_{m+2},\dots,j_{d}}-\frac{C^{U}_{d,m+1}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot\bigg[\frac{M}{2^{r_{m+1}}}+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\bigg],

where the last inequality is from the fact that

i_{m+1}-j_{m+1}\leq i_{m+1}-a^{(m+1)}_{r_{m+1}+1}<\frac{n_{m+1}}{2^{r_{m+1}}}-1-\Big(\frac{n_{m+1}}{2^{r_{m+1}+2}}-1\Big)=\frac{3}{4}\cdot\frac{n_{m+1}}{2^{r_{m+1}}}

when
r_{m+1}\neq R_{m+1}
, and

i_{m+1}-j_{m+1}\leq i_{m+1}\leq\frac{n_{m+1}}{2^{r_{m+1}}}-1<\frac{n_{m+1}}{2^{r_{m+1}}}

when
r_{m+1}=R_{m+1}
.
If we apply the same argument to the
(m+2)^{\text{th}},\dots,d^{\text{th}}
coordinates in turn, we obtain

\displaystyle(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}}

\displaystyle\geq(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta)_{i_{1},\dots,i_{d}}-(d-m)\cdot\frac{C^{U}_{d,m+1}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot\bigg[M+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\bigg]

\displaystyle>\big(C_{d}-(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}\big)\cdot\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot\bigg[M+t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq(D^{(\mathbf{1}_{m})}\theta^{*})_{j_{1},\dots,j_{d}}+\big(C_{d}-(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}\big)\cdot\frac{1}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}}\cdot t\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}.

Here, the last inequality is attributed to (
81
) and the fact that

\widetilde{C}_{d}:=C_{d}-(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}\geq B_{d}.

Hence, by Cauchy inequality, if
0\leq j^{\prime}_{1}<j_{1}\leq i_{1},\dots,0\leq j^{\prime}_{m}<j_{m}\leq i_{m}
, and if
a^{(m+1)}_{r_{m+1}+1}\leq j_{m+1}\leq i_{m+1},\dots,a^{(d)}_{r_{d}+1}\leq j_{d}\leq i_{d}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(\theta-\theta^{*})^{2}_{(1-\delta_{1})j_{1}+\delta_{1}j^{\prime}_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{m})j_{m}+\delta_{m}j^{\prime}_{m},j_{m+1},\dots,j_{d}}

\displaystyle\qquad>(j_{1}-j^{\prime}_{1})^{2}\times\cdots\times(j_{m}-j^{\prime}_{m})^{2}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}/2^{m}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}2r_{k}},

as above.
As a result,

\displaystyle t^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|\theta-\theta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\sum_{i_{1}/2<j_{1}\leq i_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{m}/2<j_{m}\leq i_{m}}\sum_{j_{m+1}=a^{(m+1)}_{r_{m+1}+1}}^{i_{m+1}}\cdots\sum_{j_{d}=a^{(d)}_{r_{d}+1}}^{i_{d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}(\theta-\theta^{*})^{2}_{(1-\delta_{1})j_{1}+\delta_{1}(i_{1}-j_{1}),\dots,(1-\delta_{m})j_{m}+\delta_{m}(i_{m}-j_{m}),j_{m+1},\dots,j_{d}}

\displaystyle>\sum_{i_{1}/2<j_{1}\leq i_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{m}/2<j_{m}\leq i_{m}}\sum_{j_{m+1}=a^{(m+1)}_{r_{m+1}+1}}^{i_{m+1}}\cdots\sum_{j_{d}=a^{(d)}_{r_{d}+1}}^{i_{d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad(2j_{1}-i_{1})^{2}\times\cdots\times(2j_{m}-i_{m})^{2}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}/2^{m}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{m}\Big[\frac{1}{6}i_{k}(i_{k}+1)(i_{k}+2)\Big]\cdot\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}\big(i_{k}-a^{(k)}_{r_{k}+1}+1\big)\cdot\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}/2^{m}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{k=1}^{m}(i_{k}+1)^{3}\cdot\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}\big(i_{k}-a^{(k)}_{r_{k}+1}+1\big)\cdot\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}/16^{m}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}2r_{k}}

\displaystyle>\prod_{k=1}^{m}\Big(\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}+1}}\Big)^{3}\cdot\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}+2}}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}/16^{m}}{\prod_{k=1}^{m}n_{k}^{2}}\cdot t^{2}\Big(\frac{2^{r_{+}}}{n}\Big)\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}2r_{k}}=\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}}{32^{m}\cdot 4^{d}}\cdot t^{2}\geq t^{2},

which leads to contradiction.
Here, for the last inequality, we use

i_{k}-a^{(k)}_{r_{k}+1}+1>\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}+1}}-1-\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}+2}}+1=\frac{n_{k}}{2^{r_{k}+2}}

for each
k=m+1,\dots,d
.

From these results, we can conclude that (
56
) and (
57
) hold with
C_{d}=C^{L}_{d,m}
and
C_{d}=C^{U}_{d,m}
, respectively, for every
\mathbf{p}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\mathbf{p}|=m
.
Thus, by induction, (
56
) and (
57
) hold for every
\mathbf{p}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{{\mathbf{0}}\}
, if we define
C_{d}=\max(\max_{m=1,\dots,d}C^{L}_{d,m},\max_{m=1,\dots,d}C^{U}_{d,m})
.
∎

C.5.2
Proof of Lemma
13

Proof of Lemma
13
.

The proof proceeds similarly to that of Lemma

11
.
We prove (
71
) and
(
72
) by induction on
|S|
, and
Lemma
16
will be repeatedly used for the proof.

We first consider the case where
|S|=d
(
S=[d]=\{1,\dots,d\}
).
Suppose, for contradiction, that there exist

\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
and

1/2^{r_{k}+1}\leq s_{k}<t_{k}\leq 1/2^{r_{k}}
for
k\in[d]
such that

\begin{bmatrix}s_{1},t_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
s_{d},t_{d}&\end{bmatrix}<-C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}},

where
C_{d}=(2\sqrt{3})^{d}=:C^{L}_{d,d}
.
By Lemma
16
, we have

\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d},v_{d}&\end{bmatrix}\leq\begin{bmatrix}s_{1},t_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
s_{d},t_{d}&\end{bmatrix}<-C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}

for every
t_{k}\leq u_{k}<v_{k}
for
k\in[d]
.
Applying Cauchy inequality, we thus obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}\Big(f\big((1-\delta_{1})v_{1}+\delta_{1}u_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{d})v_{d}+\delta_{d}u_{d}\big)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{1}{2^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}(-1)^{\delta_{1}+\cdots+\delta_{d}}\cdot f\big((1-\delta_{1})v_{1}+\delta_{1}u_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{d})v_{d}+\delta_{d}u_{d}\big)\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{2^{d}}\prod_{k=1}^{d}(v_{k}-u_{k})^{2}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d},v_{d}&\end{bmatrix}^{2}>\prod_{k=1}^{d}(v_{k}-u_{k})^{2}\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}}{2^{d}}\cdot t^{2}\cdot 2^{r_{+}}\cdot 2^{2\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}.

Since
t_{k}\leq x_{k}<t_{k}+1-x_{k}
whenever
x_{k}\in[t_{k},(t_{k}+1)/2)
,
it follows that

\displaystyle t^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|f\|_{2}^{2}\geq\int_{\prod_{k=1}^{d}[t_{k},(t_{k}+1)/2)}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}\Big(f\big((1-\delta_{1})(t_{1}+1-x_{1})+\delta_{1}x_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{d})(t_{d}+1-x_{d})+\delta_{d}x_{d}\big)\Big)^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle>\frac{C_{d}^{2}}{2^{d}}\cdot t^{2}\cdot 2^{r_{+}}\cdot 2^{2\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{d}\int_{t_{k}}^{(t_{k}+1)/2}(t_{k}+1-2x_{k})^{2}\,dx_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{d}^{2}}{12^{d}}\cdot t^{2}\cdot 2^{r_{+}}\cdot 2^{2\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{d}(1-t_{k})^{3}\geq\frac{C_{d}^{2}}{12^{d}}\cdot t^{2}=t^{2},

which is a contradiction.
Here, the last inequality follows from

1-t_{k}\geq 1-\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}}}\geq\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}}},

which holds since
r_{k}\geq 1
.

Next, suppose, for contradiction, that there exist

\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{d}
and

1/2^{r_{k}+1}\leq s_{k}<t_{k}\leq 1/2^{r_{k}}
for
k\in[d]
such that

\begin{bmatrix}s_{1},t_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
s_{d},t_{d}&\end{bmatrix}>C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}},

where
C_{d}=(4\sqrt{6})^{d}=:C^{U}_{d,d}
.
By Lemma
16
, if
u_{k}<v_{k}\leq s_{k}
for

k\in[d]
, then

\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d},v_{d}&\end{bmatrix}\geq\begin{bmatrix}s_{1},t_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
s_{d},t_{d}&\end{bmatrix}>C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}.

As in the previous case, applying Cauchy inequality gives

\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}\Big(f\big((1-\delta_{1})v_{1}+\delta_{1}u_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{d})v_{d}+\delta_{d}u_{d}\big)\Big)^{2}>\prod_{k=1}^{d}(v_{k}-u_{k})^{2}\cdot\frac{C_{d}^{2}}{2^{d}}\cdot t^{2}\cdot 2^{r_{+}}\cdot 2^{2\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}.

Since
x_{k}<s_{k}-x_{k}\leq s_{k}
for all
x_{k}\in[0,s_{k}/2)
, it follows that

\displaystyle t^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|f\|_{2}^{2}\geq\int_{\prod_{k=1}^{d}[0,s_{k}/2)}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{d}}\Big(f\big((1-\delta_{1})(s_{1}-x_{1})+\delta_{1}x_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{d})(s_{d}-x_{d})+\delta_{d}x_{d}\big)\Big)^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle>\frac{C_{d}^{2}}{2^{d}}\cdot t^{2}\cdot 2^{r_{+}}\cdot 2^{2\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{d}\int_{0}^{s_{k}/2}(s_{k}-2x_{k})^{2}\,dx_{k}=\frac{C_{d}^{2}}{12^{d}}\cdot t^{2}\cdot 2^{r_{+}}\cdot 2^{2\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{d}s_{k}^{3}\geq\frac{C_{d}^{2}}{96^{d}}\cdot t^{2}=t^{2},

which leads to contradiction.
Here, the last inequality is due to the fact that
s_{k}\geq 1/2^{r_{k}+1}
.

Now, we assume that (
71
) and
(
72
) hold with

C_{d}=C^{L}_{d,m+1}
and
C_{d}=C^{U}_{d,m+1}
,
respectively, for every subset
S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|=m+1
,
and prove that they also hold with some
C_{d}
for every subset

S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|=m
. For notational convenience, we present
the proof only for the case
S=[m]=\{1,\dots,m\}
; the other cases
follow analogously.

Suppose, for contradiction, that there exist

\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
,

1/2^{r_{k}+1}\leq s_{k}<t_{k}\leq 1/2^{r_{k}}
for
k=1,\dots,m
, and

t_{k}\in[1/2^{r_{k}+1},1/2^{r_{k}}]
for
k=m+1,\dots,d
such that

\begin{bmatrix}s_{1},t_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
s_{m},t_{m}&\\
t_{m+1}&\\
\vdots&\\
t_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}<-C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}},

where
C_{d}=(3/2)(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}+(2\sqrt{3})^{m}=:C^{L}_{d,m}
.
If
t_{k}\leq u_{k}<v_{k}
for
k=1,\dots,m
and

t_{k}\leq u_{k}\leq 1/2^{r_{k}-1}
for
k=m+1,\dots,d
, then we have

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
u_{m+1}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
t_{m+1}&;\ f\\
u_{m+2}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}+(u_{m+1}-t_{m+1})\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
t_{m+1},u_{m+1}&;\ f\\
u_{m+2}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
t_{m+1}&;\ f\\
u_{m+2}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}+\frac{3}{2^{r_{m+1}+1}}\cdot C^{U}_{d,m+1}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}r_{k}},

where the inequality follows from Lemma

16
, the induction hypothesis, and the fact that

u_{m+1}-t_{m+1}\leq\frac{1}{2^{r_{m+1}-1}}-\frac{1}{2^{r_{m+1}+1}}\leq\frac{3}{2^{r_{m+1}+1}}.

Iterating this argument for the

(m+2)^{\text{th}},\dots,d^{\text{th}}
coordinates yields

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
u_{m+1}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
t_{m+1}&\\
\vdots&\\
t_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}+\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{bmatrix}s_{1},t_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
s_{m},t_{m}&\\
t_{m+1}&\\
\vdots&\\
t_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}+\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}

\displaystyle<-\Big(C_{d}-\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}\Big)t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}.

By Cauchy inequality, it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\Big(f\big((1-\delta_{1})v_{1}+\delta_{1}u_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{m})v_{m}+\delta_{m}u_{m},u_{m+1},\dots,u_{d}\big)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad>\prod_{k=1}^{m}(v_{k}-u_{k})^{2}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}}{2^{m}}\cdot t^{2}\cdot 2^{r_{+}}\cdot 2^{2\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}},

where

\widetilde{C}_{d}:=C_{d}-\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}=(2\sqrt{3})^{m}.

Hence, integrating as before, we obtain

\displaystyle t^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|f\|_{2}^{2}\geq\int_{\prod_{k=1}^{m}[t_{k},(t_{k}+1)/2)\times\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}[t_{k},1/2^{r_{k}-1}]}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big(f\big((1-\delta_{1})(t_{1}+1-x_{1})+\delta_{1}x_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{m})(t_{m}+1-x_{m})+\delta_{m}x_{m},x_{m+1},\dots,x_{d}\big)\Big)^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle>\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}}{2^{m}}\cdot t^{2}\cdot 2^{r_{+}}\cdot 2^{2\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{m}\int_{t_{k}}^{(t_{k}+1)/2}(t_{k}+1-2x_{k})^{2}\,dx_{k}\cdot\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}\Big(\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}-1}}-t_{k}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}}{12^{m}}\cdot t^{2}\cdot 2^{r_{+}}\cdot 2^{2\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{m}(1-t_{k})^{3}\cdot\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}}}\geq\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}}{12^{m}}\cdot t^{2}=t^{2},

which is a contradiction.
Here, the second-to-last inequality uses the fact that

\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}-1}}-t_{k}\geq\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}-1}}-\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}}}=\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}}}

for
k=m+1,\dots,d
.

Next, suppose, for contradition, that there exist

\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{d}
,

1/2^{r_{k}+1}\leq s_{k}<t_{k}\leq 1/2^{r_{k}}
for
k=1,\dots,m
, and

t_{k}\in[1/2^{r_{k}+1},1/2^{r_{k}}]
for
k=m+1,\dots,d
such that

\begin{bmatrix}s_{1},t_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
s_{m},t_{m}&\\
t_{m+1}&\\
\vdots&\\
t_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}>C_{d}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}},

where
C_{d}=(3/2)(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}+(2\sqrt{6})^{m}\cdot 2^{d}=:C^{U}_{d,m}
.
For
u_{k}<v_{k}\leq s_{k}
for
k=1,\dots,m
and

1/2^{r_{k}+2}\leq u_{k}\leq t_{k}
for
k=m+1,\dots,d
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
u_{m+1}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
t_{m+1}&;\ f\\
u_{m+2}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}-(t_{m+1}-u_{m+1})\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
u_{m+1},t_{m+1}&;\ f\\
u_{m+2}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\geq\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
t_{m+1}&;\ f\\
u_{m+2}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}-\frac{3}{2^{r_{m+1}+2}}\cdot C^{U}_{d,m+1}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{(\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}r_{k})+1},

where the inequality is due to Lemma
16
, the
induction hypothesis, and the fact that

t_{m+1}-u_{m+1}\leq\frac{1}{2^{r_{m+1}}}-\frac{1}{2^{r_{m+1}+2}}\leq\frac{3}{2^{r_{m+1}+2}}.

Repeating this argument with the
(m+2)^{\text{th}},\dots,d^{\text{th}}

coordinates yields

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
u_{m+1}&\\
\vdots&\\
u_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\geq\begin{bmatrix}u_{1},v_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
u_{m},v_{m}&\\
t_{m+1}&\\
\vdots&\\
t_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}-\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}

\displaystyle\geq\begin{bmatrix}s_{1},t_{1}&\hbox{\multirowsetup; \ $f$ }\\
\vdots&\\
s_{m},t_{m}&\\
t_{m+1}&\\
\vdots&\\
t_{d}&\\
\end{bmatrix}-\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}

\displaystyle>\Big(C_{d}-\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}\Big)t\cdot 2^{r_{+}/2}\cdot 2^{\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}.

If we let

\widetilde{C}_{d}:=C_{d}-\frac{3}{2}\cdot(d-m)C^{U}_{d,m+1}=(2\sqrt{6})^{m}\cdot 2^{d},

then we have

\displaystyle t^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|f\|_{2}^{2}\geq\int_{\prod_{k=1}^{m}[0,s_{k}/2)\times\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}[1/2^{r_{k}+2},t_{k}]}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big(f\big((1-\delta_{1})(s_{1}-x_{1})+\delta_{1}x_{1},\dots,(1-\delta_{m})(s_{m}-x_{m})+\delta_{m}x_{m},x_{m+1},\dots,x_{d}\big)\Big)^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle>\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}}{2^{m}}\cdot t^{2}\cdot 2^{r_{+}}\cdot 2^{2\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{m}\int_{0}^{s_{k}/2}(s_{k}-2x_{k})^{2}\,dx_{k}\cdot\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}\Big(t_{k}-\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}+2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}}{12^{m}}\cdot t^{2}\cdot 2^{r_{+}}\cdot 2^{2\sum_{k=1}^{m}r_{k}}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{m}s_{k}^{3}\cdot\prod_{k=m+1}^{d}\frac{1}{2^{r_{k}+2}}\geq\frac{\widetilde{C}_{d}^{2}}{24^{m}\cdot 4^{d}}\cdot t^{2}=t^{2},

which is a contradiction.

For these reasons, (
71
) and
(
72
) hold with

C_{d}=C^{L}_{d,m}
and
C_{d}=C^{U}_{d,m}
,
respectively, for every subset
S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|=m
.
By induction, it follows that (
71
) and
(
72
) hold for every

\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[d]
with

C_{d}=\max(\max_{m=1,\dots,d}C^{L}_{d,m},\max_{m=1,\dots,d}C^{U}_{d,m})
.
∎

C.5.3
Proof of Lemma
14

Proof of Lemma
14
.

The proof of this lemma closely follows that of Lemma 11.18 of

[
34
]
. We therefore only describe the modifications required at
the beginning of their proof in order to establish Lemma

14
.

Suppose
f\in D(V,t)
is of the form (
9
).
For notational convenience, write
\beta_{0}=\beta_{\emptyset}
.
Here, we prove that there exists a constant
C_{d}>0
such that

|\beta_{T}|\leq C_{d}(V+t)

for every subset
T\subseteq[d]
with
|T|\leq s
.
Once this bound is established, the remainder of the proof follows exactly as
in the proof of Lemma 11.18 of
[
34
]
.

Since
V(f)\leq V
, we have

t\geq\|f\|_{2}\geq\bigg\{\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{S:0\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}\prod_{j\in S}x_{j}\bigg)^{2}\,dx\bigg\}^{1/2}-V,

which implies

\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{S:0\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}\prod_{j\in S}x_{j}\bigg)^{2}\,dx\leq(V+t)^{2}.

(82)

Now, fix
\delta\in(0,1)
, and define

A=\sum_{S:0\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}^{2}
.
Restricting the integral (
82
) to

[0,\delta]^{d}
and applying Cauchy inequality
repeatedly, we obtain

\displaystyle(V+t)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{[0,\delta]^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{S:0\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}\prod_{j\in S}x_{j}\bigg)^{2}\,dx\geq\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\emptyset}^{2}\delta^{d}-\int_{[0,\delta]^{d}}\bigg(\sum_{S:0<|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}\prod_{j\in S}x_{j}\bigg)^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\emptyset}^{2}\delta^{d}-A\int_{[0,\delta]^{d}}\sum_{S:0<|S|\leq s}\prod_{j\in S}x_{j}^{2}\,dx=\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\emptyset}^{2}\delta^{d}-C_{d}\delta^{d+2}\cdot A,

and therefore,

\beta_{\emptyset}^{2}\leq 2(V+t)^{2}\delta^{-d}+C_{d}\delta^{2}\cdot A.

Next, assume that for every
T\subseteq[d]
with
|T|<m\leq s
, we have

\beta_{T}^{2}\leq C_{d}(V+t)^{2}\delta^{-d}+C_{d}\delta^{2}\cdot A.

(83)

We now show that the same bound, possibly with a different constant
C_{d}
,
also holds for all subsets
T\subseteq[d]
with
|T|=m
.
Without loss of generality, it suffices to consider the case

T=[m]=\{1,\dots,m\}
, since the argument is identical for any
other subset of size
m
.

Restricting the integral (
82
)
to
[0,1]^{m}\times[0,\delta]^{d-m}
and applying Cauchy inequality
yields

\displaystyle(V+t)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{[0,1]^{m}\times[0,\delta]^{d-m}}\bigg(\sum_{S:0\leq|S|\leq s}\beta_{S}\prod_{j\in S}x_{j}\bigg)^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{3}\beta_{[m]}^{2}\delta^{d-m}-\int_{[0,1]^{m}\times[0,\delta]^{d-m}}\bigg(\sum_{S\subsetneq[m]}\beta_{S}\prod_{j\in S}x_{j}\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S:0\leq|S|\leq s\\
S\nsubseteq[m]\end{subarray}}\beta_{S}\prod_{j\in S}x_{j}\bigg)^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{3}\beta_{[m]}^{2}\delta^{d-m}-\bigg(\sum_{S\subsetneq[m]}\beta_{S}^{2}\bigg)\cdot\int_{[0,1]^{m}\times[0,\delta]^{d-m}}\sum_{S\subsetneq[m]}\prod_{j\in S}x_{j}^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad-A\int_{[0,1]^{m}\times[0,\delta]^{d-m}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S:0\leq|S|\leq s\\
S\nsubseteq[m]\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in S}x_{j}^{2}\,dx.

By the induction hypothesis, we thus have

\displaystyle(V+t)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{3}\beta_{[m]}^{2}\delta^{d-m}-\sum_{S\subsetneq[m]}\big(C_{d}(V+t)^{2}\delta^{-d}+C_{d}\delta^{2}\cdot A\big)\cdot\delta^{d-m}-C_{d}\delta^{d-m+2}\cdot A

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{3}\beta_{[m]}^{2}\delta^{d-m}-C_{d}(V+t)^{2}\delta^{-m}-C_{d}\delta^{d-m+2}\cdot A.

Rearranging terms, we obtain

\beta_{[m]}^{2}\leq 3(V+t)^{2}\delta^{m-d}+C_{d}(V+t)^{2}\delta^{-d}+C_{d}\delta^{2}\cdot A\leq C_{d}(V+t)^{2}\delta^{-d}+C_{d}\delta^{2}\cdot A.

This proves that the bound (
83
) holds for
T=[m]
,
and hence, for all
T\subseteq[d]
with
|T|=m
.
By induction, it follows that the bound (
83
)
holds for every
T\subseteq[d]
with
|T|\leq s
.

Summing the bounds (
83
) over all subsets

T\subseteq[d]
with
|T|\leq s
, we obtain

A\leq C_{d}(V+t)^{2}\delta^{-d}+C_{d}\delta^{2}\cdot A.

Hence,

A\leq\frac{C_{d}(V+t)^{2}\delta^{-d}}{1-C_{d}\delta^{2}}

provided
C_{d}\delta^{2}<1
.
Choosing
\delta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small and substituting this bound
back into (
83
), we obtain

\beta_{T}^{2}\leq C_{d}(V+t)^{2}

for all
T\subseteq[d]
with
|T|\leq s
, as desired.
∎
